, which is a quasi-projective variety. To resolve the second issue, we will use instead that the contraction
f:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{X}}
is projective. Finally, the third issue is addressed in Remark
5.1
.

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration.
As
\pi
is a smoothing of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
, there is a natural log structure on
\mathcal{X}_{0}
, log smooth over the standard log point – see e.g.
[
93
, §3.2]

for foundational aspects of log structures on formal schemes.
We denote by
\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger}
the corresponding log scheme. Its tropicalization
\Sigma(\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger})
is the cone
(C(P),C(\mathscr{P}))
over the dual intersection complex
(P,\mathscr{P})
.

We will define the structure constants
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})
of the mirror algebra to
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
using moduli spaces of stable punctured maps to
\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger}
.
A punctured map to
\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger}
is a log morphism
\nu^{\dagger}:C^{\dagger}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger}
, where
C
is a log curve satisfying the conditions in
[
2
, Definition 2.13]
.
In particular, the scheme
C
underlying
C^{\dagger}
is a projective curve with at worst nodal singularities, and there are distinguished points
x_{i}
on
C
called punctured points. Moreover, each punctured point
x_{i}
has a contact order
p_{i}
, which is either an element of
C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}
or of
-C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}
. A punctured point
x_{i}
is called a marked point if
p_{i}\in C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}
. Finally, a punctured map is called stable if the underlying map of schemes
\nu:C\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{0}
is a stable map.
We refer to
[
2
]
for the details of the theory of stable punctured maps to a log scheme.

Remark 5.1
.

The theory of punctured maps is developed in
[
2
]

under the assumption that the target has a log structure defined with respect to the Zariski topology (and not with respect to the étale topology). Correspondingly, the mirror construction of Gross–Siebert
[
48
,
49
]
is formulated under the assumption that the central fiber is simple normal crossing instead of just normal crossing. If the open Kulikov surface
\mathcal{X}_{0}
is not simple normal crossing, there is an edge
e
in
\mathscr{P}
connecting a vertex
v
to itself. Taking the cone
C(\widetilde{\mathscr{P}})
over a rational triangulation
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}
refining
\mathscr{P}
and containing as vertices some rational points in the interior of every such edges
e
defines a log blow-up
\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}
of
\mathcal{X}
, so that the central fiber
\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}_{0}
is now simple normal crossing, and the Gross–Siebert construction (with the modifications described below to deal with the non-compactness of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
) applies to such a degeneration
\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow\Delta
. Since this log blow-up is harmless for all our arguments, we will no longer mention it explicitly for the remaining of this paper.

A type
\tau
of punctured maps is a choice of a curve class
\beta\in NE(\mathcal{X}_{0})
, a genus, a number of punctured points, and for each punctured point a choice of contact order. For every type
\tau
, there is a moduli space
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger},\tau)
of stable punctured maps to
\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger}
of type
\tau
. By
[
2
]
,
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger},\tau)
is a Deligne-Mumford stack, endowed with a natural log structure.

Let
r\in C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}\setminus\{0\}
, and denote by
Z_{r}
the stratum of
\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger}
corresponding to the smallest cone of
C(P)
containing
r
. As in
[
48
, §3.1]
, one can define a moduli space
\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger},r)
of punctures in
\mathcal{X}_{0}
with contact order
-r
, which is a log stack with underlying stack
Z_{r}\times B\mathbb{G}_{m}
. As in Definition
[
48
, 3.5]
, for every
type
\tau
of genus zero containing a punctured point
x_{\mathrm{out}}
with contact order
-r
.
there is a log evaluation morphism at
x_{\mathrm{out}}

\mathrm{ev}_{x_{\mathrm{out}}}:\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger},\tau)\longrightarrow\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger},r)\,.

By
[
48
, Proposition 3.8]
, a choice of a point
z
in the interior of
Z_{r}

induces a log morphism

B\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}\longrightarrow\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger},r)\,,

with underlying morphism the inclusion
z\times B\mathbb{G}_{m}\subset Z_{r}\times B\mathbb{G}_{m}
, where
B\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}
is the stack
B\mathbb{G}_{m}
endowed with the restriction of the divisorial log structure on
[\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
defined by
[0/B\mathbb{G}_{m}]\subset[\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
.
Finally, one defines

(5.2)

\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger},\tau,z):=\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger},\tau)\times_{\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger},r)}B\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}\,,

where the fiber product is taken in the category of log stacks.

Every log scheme
X
has an associated Artin fan
\mathcal{A}_{X}

[
3
, Proposition 3.1.1]
. The Artin fan is a 0-dimensional log stack, which captures the combinatorial aspects of the log structure on
X
. One can define as in (
5.2
) a moduli space
\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger}},\tau,z)

of prestable punctured maps to the Artin fan
\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger}}
, and there is a natural forgetful morphism

\phi:\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger},\tau,z)\longrightarrow\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger}},\tau,z)\,.

Denoting by
p:C\rightarrow\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger},\tau,z)
the universal domain curve, and by
\nu:C\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger}
, then, as in
[
2
, Proposition 3.12]
,
Rp_{\star}(\nu^{\star}(T_{\widetilde{X}^{\dagger}}|_{\mathcal{X}_{0}})(-x_{\mathrm{out}}))

is a 2-term perfect obstruction theory relative to
\phi
of relative virtual dimension zero, where
T_{\widetilde{X}^{\dagger}}|_{\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger}}
is the restriction to
\mathcal{X}_{0}
of the log tangent bundle of
\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}^{\dagger}
.

If all the punctured points of the type
\tau
apart from
x_{\mathrm{out}}
are marked points, that is, with contact orders in
C(P)
, then, as in
[
2
, Proposition 3.19]
,
\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger}},\tau,z)
is pure-dimensional of dimension
n-2
, and so
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger},\tau,z)
carries a virtual fundamental class

(5.3)

[\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger},\tau,z)]^{\mathrm{virt}}

of dimension
n-2
, where
n
is the number of marked points in
\tau
.
In order to define invariants from these virtual fundamental classes, one needs to know that the moduli spaces
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger},\tau,z)
are proper. This properness is not immediate as
\mathcal{X}_{0}
is not projective, but nevertheless holds by the following result. The proof of properness uses in an essential way the existence of a contraction
f:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{X}}
for an open Kulikov degeneration
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
.

Theorem 5.2
.

Let
r\in C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}\setminus\{0\}
and
\tau
be a genus zero type of punctured maps to
\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger}
, of class
\beta\in NE(\mathcal{X}_{0}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{0})\cong NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
,
with a punctured point
x_{\mathrm{out}}
of contact order
-r
, and whose all other punctured points are marked points. Then, for every point
z
in the interior of
Z_{r}
, the moduli space
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger},\tau,z)
is proper.

Proof.

Let
\nu\colon C\to\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger}
be a punctured map defining a point in the moduli space
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger},\tau,z)
. As the class
\beta\in NE(\mathcal{X}_{0})
of the image
\nu(C)
is contained in
NE(\mathcal{X}_{0}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{0})
, it follows that
\nu(C)
is contracted to a point under the map
f\colon\mathcal{X}\to\overline{\mathcal{X}}
. By definition of the moduli space
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger},\tau,z)
, the image
\nu(C)
contains the point
z
. Thus, we have
f(\nu(C))=\{f(z)\}
, and so
\nu(C)\subset f^{-1}(\{f(z)\})
. Note that for
r\neq 0
we have
Z_{r}\subset\mathcal{X}_{0}
and since
z\in Z_{r}
we have
z\in\mathcal{X}_{0}
. Therefore, we have
f(z)\in\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{0}
, and so

f^{-1}(\{f(z)\})
is actually a subvariety of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
. Since every punctured map defining a point in the moduli space
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger},\tau,z)
factors through the subvariety
V:=f^{-1}(\{f(z)\})
of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
, it follows that

\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger},\tau,z)\simeq\mathcal{M}(V^{\dagger},\tau,z)
,
where
V^{\dagger}
is the log scheme obtained by restricting the log structure of
\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger}
to
V=f^{-1}(\{f(z)\})
.
Since
f\colon\mathcal{X}\to\overline{\mathcal{X}}
is projective, the fiber
V=f^{-1}(\{f(z)\})
of
f
is a projective subvariety of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
, and so the properness of
\mathcal{M}(V^{\dagger},\tau,z)

follows by
[
2
]
.
∎

We can now define the structure constants
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})
in our setup.
For every
p,q\in C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}
,
r\in C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}\setminus\{0\}
and
\beta\in NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
, we denote by
\tau_{pqr}^{\beta}
the type of punctured maps to
\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{0}^{\dagger}
of genus zero, class
\beta
, with two marked points with contact order
p
and
q
and one punctured point with contact order
-r
. As the type
\tau_{pqr}^{\beta}
has
n=2
marked points, the virtual fundamental class
[\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger},\tau,z)]^{\mathrm{virt}}
in (
5.3
) has dimension
n-2=0
. Moreover, by Theorem
5.2
, the moduli space
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger},\tau,z)
is proper and so the degree of
0
-cycles on
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger},\tau,z)
is well-defined.

Definition 5.3
.

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration. For every
p,q,r\in C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}
and
\beta\in NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
, the
structure constant

N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})\in\mathbb{Q}
is defined by

N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})=\begin{cases}\mathrm{deg}[\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger},\tau_{pqr}^{\beta},z)]^{\mathrm{virt}}&\quad\text{if}\,\,\,r\neq 0\\
1&\quad\text{if}\,\,\,p=q=r=0\,\,\,\text{and}\,\,\,\beta=0\\
0&\quad\text{otherwise.}\\
\end{cases}

Definition 5.4
.

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration.
For every monoid ideal
I\subset NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
such that

NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})\setminus I
is finite, the mirror algebra
R_{I}(\mathcal{X})

is the free
S_{I}(\mathcal{X})=\mathbb{C}[NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})]/I
-module

(5.4)

R_{I}(\mathcal{X})=\bigoplus_{p\in C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}}S_{I}(\mathcal{X})\vartheta_{p}\,,

endowed with the
S_{I}(\mathcal{X})
-linear product given
for every
p,q\in C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}
by

(5.5)

\vartheta_{p}\cdot\vartheta_{q}=\sum_{r\in C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}}\sum_{\beta\in NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})\setminus I}N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})t^{\beta}\vartheta_{r}

where the structure constants
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})\in\mathbb{Q}
are as in Definition
5.3
.

We call the basis
\{\vartheta_{p}\}_{p\in C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}}
of
R_{I}(\mathcal{X})
the
theta basis
of the mirror algebra.

Theorem 5.5
.

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration.
For every monoid ideal
I\subset NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
such that

NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})\setminus I
is finite, the mirror algebra
R_{I}(\mathcal{X})
is a commutative, associative
S_{I}(\mathcal{X})
-algebra with unit given by
\vartheta_{0}
.

Proof.

All the arguments in the proof of
[
48
, Theorem 1.9]

for projective degenerations
g:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow S
as in §
5.1
can be adapted to the set-up of open Kulikov degenerations to prove Theorem
5.5
.
Indeed, the proof of
[
48
, Theorem 1.9]
for a projective degeneration

g:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow S

is based on the study of the moduli spaces
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X},\tau,z)
, where
\tau
is a genus zero type of punctured maps with three marked points and an additional punctured point. For an open Kulikov degeneration
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
, the corresponding moduli spaces
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger},\tau,z)
are proper by Theorem
5.2
and so the non-compactness of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
is not an issue.
∎

5.3.
Structure constants from the canonical scattering diagram

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration, with tropicalization
(C(P),C(\mathscr{P}))
.
By
[
49
]
, the structure constants
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})
can be computed in terms of broken lines in the canonical scattering diagram for
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{X}_{0})
. The canonical scattering diagram

\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{X}}
is a collection of walls in
C(P)
, which are cones
C(W)
over line segments
W
of rational slopes contained in 2-dimensional faces
\sigma_{W}
of
\mathscr{P}
in
P
. Every wall
C(W)
is endowed with a kink
\kappa_{W}\in NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
.
If
W
is contained in an internal edge
e
of
\mathscr{P}
, then the kink
\kappa_{W}
is equal to the class
[C_{e}]
of the corresponding proper curve
C_{e}
in the double locus of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
. Else, we have
\kappa_{W}=0
. Each wall
C(W)
is also endowed with a power series
f_{W}\in\mathbb{Q}[\Lambda_{W}][\![NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})]\!]
, where
\Lambda_{W}
is the 1-dimensional lattice of integral tangent vectors to
W
. The power series
f_{W}

is a generating series of genus
0
punctured
Gromov–Witten invariants
N_{\tau}
,
indexed by wall types
\tau
and virtually counting
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-curves
,

(5.6)

f_{W}=\exp\left(\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{W}}k_{\tau}N_{\tau}z^{m_{\tau}}t^{\beta_{\tau}}\right)\,,

where
\mathcal{T}_{W}
is the set of wall types
\tau
whose leg contains
W
– see
[
49
]
for details.

A broken line is a piecewise linear embedding

b:(-\infty,0]\rightarrow C(P)
, oriented by increasing values of the parameter, with finitely many domains of linearity
b_{j}
, each one contained in a 3-dimensional face of
C(\mathscr{P})
, and decorated with a monomial
c_{b_{j}}z^{m_{b_{j}}}
, where
c_{b_{j}}\in\mathbb{Q}[\![NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})]\!]
and
m_{b_{j}}\in\Lambda_{b_{j}}
, where
\Lambda_{b_{j}}
is the 1-dimensional lattice of integral tangent vectors to
b_{j}
, and with
-m_{b_{j}}
pointing in the direction of the broken line. For the initial domain of linearity
b_{\mathrm{in}}
, we have

c_{b_{\mathrm{in}}}=1
and
m_{b_{\mathrm{in}}}
is called the initial direction of the broken line. Moreover, a bending from a domain of linearity
b_{j}
to a domain of linearity
b_{j+1}
occurs when the broken line crosses a wall
W
, and the monomial

c_{b_{j+1}}z^{m_{b_{j+1}}}
is a monomial in the expansion of

(5.7)

c_{b_{j}}z^{m_{b_{j}}}\left(t^{\kappa_{W}}f_{W}\right)^{\langle n_{W},m_{b_{j}}\rangle}\,,

where
n_{W}
is the primitive normal vector to
C(W)
, positive on the half-space containing
b_{j}
.
Finally, we denote by
b_{\mathrm{out}}
the final domain of linearity of
b
, and
c_{b_{\mathrm{out}}}z^{m_{b_{\mathrm{out}}}}
the corresponding monomial.
By
[
49
, Theorem 6.1]
, for every
p,q,r\in C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}
, we then have

(5.8)

N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})=\sum_{b,b^{\prime}}c_{b_{\mathrm{out}}}c_{b^{\prime}_{\mathrm{out}}}\,,

where the sum is over pairs of broken lines
b
,
b^{\prime}

of initial directions
p
,
q
, ending at a common general point close to
r\in C(P)
, and with
m_{b_{\mathrm{out}}}+m_{b_{\mathrm{out}}^{\prime}}=r
.
One can also work only on
P
at height 1 in
C(P)
by working with the radial projections on
P
of the broken lines, called jagged paths

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration. Then, for every
p,q,r\in C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}
and
\beta\in NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
, the structure constants
N_{pqr}^{\beta}
only depends on the log central fiber
\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger}
.

Proof.

As reviewed above, by
[
49
, Theorem 6.1]
,
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})
can be computed from the canonical scattering diagram, which only depends on
\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger}
by
[
49
, Proposition 3.7]
.
∎

Remark 5.7
.

Lemma
5.6
does not immediately follows from Definition
5.3
. Indeed, even if
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger},\tau,z)
only depends on
\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger}
, the definition of the relative obstruction theory, and so also the definition of the virtual fundamental class
[\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger},\tau,z)]^{\mathrm{virt}}
, depend a priori on
\mathcal{X}
.

6.
Polarized mirrors of open Kulikov degenerations

After classifying in §
6.1
the elementary flops between quasi-projective open Kulikov degenerations, we study in §
6.2
how the structure constants
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})
of the mirror algebra change under these flops.
This is used in §
6.3
to prove a key finiteness property: the mirror algebra of a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
can be defined over
\mathbb{C}[NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})]
, and not just over the Artinian rings
\mathbb{C}[NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})]/I
.
In §
6.4
-
6.5
, we define the secondary fan

\mathrm{Sec}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
as a coarsening of the Mori fan
\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
. We show that there is a natural stacky fan
\mathcal{S}ec(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
with underlying fan

\mathrm{Sec}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
, and that the Proj of the mirror algebras of the various projective crepant resolutions of
\overline{\mathcal{X}}
glue and extend to a polarized mirror family
(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{sec}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{sec}}+\epsilon\,\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{sec}})\rightarrow\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{sec}}
over the toric Deligne–Mumford stack
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{sec}}
with fan
\mathrm{Sec}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
.

6.1.
Elementary flops

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov
degeneration. By Proposition
3.36
,

\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{X}}
is a Mori dream space, and so
\mathcal{X}
admits finitely many small
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial modifications over
\overline{\mathcal{X}}
.
Let
b:\mathcal{X}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
be such a small
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial modification of
\mathcal{X}
over
\overline{\mathcal{X}}
. Then, both

\mathrm{Nef}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
and
\mathrm{Nef}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
are maximal dimensional cones of the Mori fan
\mathrm{MF}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
. We say that
b
is
elementary

if the intersection
\mathrm{Nef}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})\cap\mathrm{Nef}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}})
is a common codimension one face of
\mathrm{Nef}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
and
\mathrm{Nef}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
, that is, if the face
NE(b)
of
NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
spanned by the curves contracted by
b
is an extremal ray.
The following result classifies the possible elementary
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial modifications.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration, and let
b:\mathcal{X}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
be an elementary small
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial modification of
\mathcal{X}
over
\overline{\mathcal{X}}
. Then, the exceptional locus of
b
is a smooth rational curve
C\subset\mathcal{X}_{0}
, such that either

i)

C
is an internal exceptional curve of an irreducible component of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
, or

ii)

C=V_{i}\cap V_{j}
is an irreducible component of the double locus of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
, intersection of two irreducible components
V_{i}
,
V_{j}
of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
, and such that
\deg N_{C|V_{i}}=\deg N_{C|V_{j}}=-1
.

In both cases, the normal bundle to
C
in
\mathcal{X}
is
N_{C|\mathcal{X}}=\mathcal{O}_{C}(-1)\oplus\mathcal{O}_{C}(-1)
, and
b
is the standard
(-1,-1)
-flop of
C
. In particular,
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\rightarrow\Delta
is also a quasi-projective open
Kulikov degeneration.

Proof.

As
\mathcal{X}_{0}
is generic as in Definition
3.19
, and so does not contain any internal
(-2)
-curve, it follows from the proof of
[
86
, Lemma 3.2]
that the connected components of the exceptional locus of
b
are either as in i) or ii).
Hence, to prove Lemma
6.1
, it is enough to show that the exceptional locus of
b
is in fact connected. Assume that
C
and
C^{\prime}
are two curves as in i) or ii) with proportional classes. Curves as in i) have negative intersection with exactly one irreducible component of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
, whereas curves as in ii) have negative intersection with exactly two irreducible components of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
. Hence, either both
C
and
C^{\prime}
are as in i), or both
C
and
C^{\prime}
are as in ii). In the latter case, we have
C=C^{\prime}=V_{i}\cap V_{j}
, where
V_{i}
and
V_{j}
are the two irreducible components of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
with
C\cdot V_{i}<0
and
C\cdot V_{j}<0
. In the former case, both
C
and
C^{\prime}
are internal exceptional curves in the same irreducible component
V_{i}
of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
, the one such that
C\cdot V_{i}<0
, and intersect the same irreducible component
V_{i}\cap V_{j}
of
\partial V_{i}
:
V_{j}
is the unique irreducible component of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
such that
C.V_{i}>0
. Hence, the line bundle
\mathcal{O}_{V_{i}}(C-C^{\prime})
is trivial in restriction to every irreducible component of
\partial V_{i}
, and so, by the description of
\mathrm{Pic}(\mathcal{X}_{0})
given by Lemma
3.10
, defines a line bundle on
\mathcal{X}_{0}
. By Lemma
3.10
, we also have
H^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{0},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{0}})=0
, and so this line bundle formally deforms into a line bundle
L
on
\mathcal{X}
. If
C\neq C^{\prime}
, then
L\cdot C=-1
, whereas
L\cdot C^{\prime}=1
, and so, as the classes
C
and
C^{\prime}
are assumed to be proportional,
C=-C^{\prime}
, in contradiction with the strict convexity of the cone of curves
NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
.
∎

Following
[
7
, Definition 7.15]
, we call
M1 flop
a small
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial modification as in Lemma
6.1
i), that is the flop of an internal exceptional curve, and
M2 flop
a small
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial modification as in Lemma
6.1
ii), that is the flop of an exceptional curve contained in the double locus. In restriction to the central fiber
\mathcal{X}_{0}
, M1 and M2 flops act like the M1 and M2 modifications of open Kulikov surfaces introduced in §
3.3
.
Note that M1 and M2 flops are often referred to as Type 1 and Type 2 modifications in the literature
[
87
, pg. 12]
.

6.2.
Structure constants under flops

In this section, we describe the behavior of
structure constants
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})
under M1-M2 flops. Parallel results are obtained in
[
44
]

in the context of Type III degenerations of K3 surfaces.

Since M1 and M2 flops are isomorphisms in codimension one, the Picard group of an open Kulikov degeneration remains invariant under such flops. Thus we have a natural isomorphism

h\colon N_{1}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})\longrightarrow N_{1}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})

where
N_{1}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
denotes the group of numerical equivalence classes of one cycles in
\mathcal{X}
, whose irreducible components are curves contracted to a point under the map
f\colon\mathcal{X}\to\overline{\mathcal{X}}
. Here, by the definition of M1 and M2 flops, the image of the flopping curve class
[C]\in N_{1}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
under the map in Equation (
6.2
) is
-[C^{\prime}]\in N_{1}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
,
where
C^{\prime}
is the flopped curve.
Moreover, there is a natural identification between the integral points of the tropicalization
C(P)
of
\mathcal{X}\to\Delta
and the integral points of the tropicalization
C(P^{\prime})
of
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\to\Delta
.

Theorem 6.2
.

Let
\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
and
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\to\Delta
be two quasi-projective open Kulikov degenerations differing by an M1 flop. Then, for every
p,q,r\in C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}\cong C(P^{\prime})_{\mathbb{Z}}
and
\beta\in NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
, we have the equality
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})=N_{pqr}^{h(\beta)}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime})
,
where
h
is the isomorphism given in Equation (
6.2
).

Proof.

It follows from the properness of moduli spaces given by Theorem
5.2
that the results proved in
[
40
, §8]

on punctured Gromov–Witten invariants of Type III degenerations of K3 surfaces also hold by the same arguments for the punctured Gromov–Witten
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})
of quasi-projective open Kulikov degenerations.
By
[
40
, Theorem 8.15]
, the punctured Gromov–Witten invariants
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})
can be decomposed as a product of punctured Gromov–Witten invariants of the irreducible components
(X^{i},\partial X^{i})
of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
and of toric surfaces (appearing as irreducible components of a large enough base change of
\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
). On the other hand, the M1 flop
\mathcal{X}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
induces an isomorphism between
\mathcal{X}_{0}
and
\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\prime}
, except in restriction to the irreducible component
X^{v}
containing the flopping curve
C
, and to the only other irreducible components
X^{v^{\prime}}
intersecting
C
. Hence, Theorem
6.2
reduces to a local statement comparing punctured Gromov–Witten invariants of
X^{v}\cup X^{v^{\prime}}
under the blow-down of
C
in
X_{v}
, and the blow-up of the point
C\cap X^{v^{\prime}}
in
X^{v^{\prime}}
. Finally, this statement follows from
[
40
, Theorem 8.16]
describing the change of the punctured Gromov–Witten invariants of a log Calabi–Yau surfaces under internal blow-up. We refer to
[
44
]
for more details.
∎

Remark 6.3
.

An M1 flop induces an isomorphism at the level of polyhedral complexes
(P,\mathscr{P})
. At the level of the integral affine structure with singularities on
P
,
a focus-focus singularity moves along its monodromy invariant from the vertex
v
, corresponding to the component
X^{v}
containing the flopping curve
C
, to the vertex
v^{\prime}
, corresponding to the component
X^{v^{\prime}}
containing the flopped curve
C^{\prime}
.

Let
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration obtained from a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration
\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
by an M2 flop along a curve
C
in
\mathcal{X}
. The curve
C
corresponds to an edge
e_{C}
of
\mathscr{P}
, which is a diagonal in a quadrilateral
Q
. The polyhedral decomposition
\mathscr{P}^{\prime}
of
P
defined by
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
is obtained from
\mathscr{P}
by flip of
e_{C}
in
Q
, that is, by removing the diagonal
e_{C}
of
Q
and introducing the opposite diagonal
e_{C^{\prime}}
of
Q
.
Let
c_{Q}\in C(\mathscr{P})_{\mathbb{Z}}
be the integral primitive generator of the ray in
C(\mathscr{P})
passing through the intersection point of
e_{C}
and
e_{C^{\prime}}
in
P\subset C(P)
. Geometrically,
c_{Q}
is the divisorial valuation defined by the exceptional divisor
E
of the blow-up
\mathcal{Z}
of the ordinary double point obtained by contracting
C
.
Denote by
\widetilde{C(\mathscr{P})}
the dual intersection complex of
\mathcal{Z}
, that is, the cone complex obtained from
C(\mathscr{P})
by adding the cone over
e_{C^{\prime}}
. Let

(6.1)

\varphi_{E}:C(P)\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}

be the unique piecewise-linear function, linear on each cone of
\widetilde{C(\mathscr{P})}
, such that
\varphi_{E}(p)=0
for every
p\in P_{\mathbb{Z}}
and
\varphi_{E}(c_{Q})=1
.

Theorem 6.4
.

Let
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration obtained from a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration
\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
by an M2 flop along a curve
C
in
\mathcal{X}
.
Then, for every
p,q,r\in C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}\cong C(P^{\prime})_{\mathbb{Z}}
and
\beta\in NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
, we have the equality

N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})=N_{pqr}^{h(\beta)+m_{pqr}[C]}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime})\,,

where
h
is the isomorphism given in Equation (
6.2
), and

m_{pqr}=\varphi_{E}(p)+\varphi_{E}(q)-\varphi_{E}(r)\,,

where
\varphi_{E}
is the PL function as in Equation (
6.1
).

Proof.

The M2 flop is defined by a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\,.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

As
\mathcal{Z}
is a log modification of
\mathcal{X}
,
induced by the subdivision
\widetilde{C(\mathscr{P})}
of
C(\mathscr{P})
,
we have by
[
56
, §10]

N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\widetilde{\beta}\in NE(\mathcal{Z}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})\\
p_{\star}\widetilde{\beta}=\beta\end{subarray}}N_{pqr}^{\widetilde{\beta}}(\mathcal{Z})\,.

In fact, arguing as in the proof of
[
40
, Lemma 8.7]
, there exists a unique
\widetilde{\beta}
such that
N_{pqr}^{\widetilde{\beta}}(\mathcal{Z})\neq 0
, and so we have
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})=N_{pqr}^{\widetilde{\beta}}(\mathcal{Z})
.
Similarly, we have
N_{pqr}^{\widetilde{\beta}}(\mathcal{Z})=N_{pqr}^{p^{\prime}_{\star}\widetilde{\beta}}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime})
.
Hence, in order to prove Theorem
6.4
, it is enough to show that
p^{\prime}_{\star}\widetilde{\beta}=h(\beta)+m_{pqr}C
.
This formula holds since, by geometry of the flop, we have
p^{\prime}_{\star}\widetilde{\beta}=h(\beta)+(E\cdot\widetilde{\beta})C
, and
we have
E\cdot\widetilde{\beta}=\varphi_{E}(p)+\varphi_{E}(q)-\varphi_{E}(r)
by Lemma
6.7
.
∎

Let
\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration, with tropicalization
(C(P),C(\mathscr{P}))
. As in
[
45
, Definition 1.8]
, let
\varphi
be the
N_{1}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})_{\mathbb{R}}
-valued multivalued PL function on
C(P)
with kink
[C]\in NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
across the codimension one face of
C(\mathscr{P})
corresponding to the irreducible component
[C]
of the double locus of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
. By
[
45
, Construction 1.14]
,
\varphi
determines a cone complex
(C(P)_{\varphi},C(\mathscr{P})_{\varphi})
, and a map of cone complexes
\pi:(C(P)_{\varphi},C(\mathscr{P})_{\varphi})\rightarrow(C(P),C(\mathscr{P}))
such that
\pi:C(P)_{\varphi}\rightarrow C(P)
is a

N_{1}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})_{\mathbb{R}}
-torsor, and
\varphi
can naturally be viewed as a section of
\pi
.

Let
\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
and
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\rightarrow\Delta
be two quasi-projective open Kulikov degenerations with
\overline{\mathcal{X}}=\overline{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}
, and
\varphi
and
\varphi^{\prime}
the corresponding multivalued PL functions. It follows from
[
50
, §2.1]

that we have a natural homeomorphism
C(P)_{\varphi}\simeq C(P^{\prime})_{\varphi^{\prime}}
, preserving the integral points, but not the cone decompositions
C(\mathscr{P})_{\varphi}
and
C(\mathscr{P}^{\prime})_{\varphi^{\prime}}
in general. In particular, the difference
\varphi^{\prime}-\varphi
can be viewed as an ordinary
N_{1}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})_{\mathbb{R}}
-valued function on
C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}\simeq C(P^{\prime})_{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Figure 6.1.
Change of the polyhedral decomposition
\mathscr{P}
under a M2 flop on the left. The function
\varphi_{E}
on the right.

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration obtained from a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration
\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
by an M2 flop along a curve
C
in
\mathcal{X}
. Then, for every
p\in C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}\simeq C(P^{\prime})_{\mathbb{Z}}
, we have

\varphi^{\prime}(p)-\varphi(p)=\varphi_{E}(p)[C]\,,

where
\varphi_{E}
is the PL function as in Equation (
6.1
).

Proof.

Recall that
\mathscr{P}^{\prime}
is obtained from
\mathscr{P}
by flipping a diagonal
e_{C}
of a quadrilateral
Q
into the opposite diagonal
e_{C^{\prime}}
– see Figure
6.1
. Away from
Q
, the decompositions
\mathscr{P}
and
\mathscr{P}^{\prime}
coincide, we have
\varphi_{E}=0
, and so we clearly have
\varphi^{\prime}=\varphi+\varphi_{E}[C]
.
Hence, it is enough to show that
\varphi+\varphi_{E}[C]
has the same kinks as
\varphi^{\prime}
in restriction to a neighborhood of
Q
.
Identifying
Q
with the standard square of integral size one, the restriction of
\varphi_{E}
to
Q
is given as in Figure
6.1
, and so has kinks
1
across the sides of
Q
and kinks
-1
across the 4 connected components of
(e_{C}\cup e_{C^{\prime}})\setminus(e_{C}\cap e_{C^{\prime}})
. In particular, given a side
s
of
Q
corresponding to an irreducible component
C_{s}
of the irreducible component of the double locus of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
, the kink of
\varphi+\varphi_{E}[C]
across
s
is
[C_{s}]+[C]=[C_{s}]-[C^{\prime}]
, which is exactly the class of the strict transform of
C_{s}
under the M2 flop, and so the kink of
\varphi^{\prime}
across
s
.
Finally, the kink of
\varphi+\varphi_{E}[C]
across
e_{C}
(resp.
e_{C^{\prime}}
) is
[C]-[C]=0

(resp .
0-[C]=[C^{\prime}]
), which is exactly the corresponding kink of
\varphi^{\prime}
.
∎

Theorem 6.6
.

Let
\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
and
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\rightarrow\Delta
be two quasi-projective open Kulikov degenerations with
\overline{\mathcal{X}}=\overline{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}
.
Then, for every
p,q,r\in C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}\cong C(P^{\prime})_{\mathbb{Z}}
and
\beta\in NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
, we have the equality

N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})=N_{pqr}^{h(\beta)+n_{pqr}}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime})\,,

where

n_{pqr}=(\varphi^{\prime}(p)-\varphi(p))+(\varphi^{\prime}(q)-\varphi(q))-(\varphi^{\prime}(r)-\varphi(r))\in N_{1}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})\simeq N_{1}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})\,.

Proof.

As we have
\overline{\mathcal{X}}=\overline{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}
, it follows that
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
are related by a small modification over
\overline{\mathcal{X}}
, that is, by a composition of elementary modifications, which, by Lemma
6.1
, are either M1 or M2 flops. Given three quasi-projective open Kulikov degenerations
\mathcal{X}
,
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
, and
\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime}

with
\overline{\mathcal{X}}=\overline{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}=\overline{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime\prime}
, we have
\varphi^{\prime\prime}-\varphi=(\varphi^{\prime\prime}-\varphi^{\prime})+(\varphi^{\prime}-\varphi)
, and so
it is enough to prove Theorem
6.6
for
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
related either by an M1 or an M2 flop.

If
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{X}
are related by an M1 flop, then
\mathcal{X}_{0}
and
\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\prime}
have the same double curves. In particular, we have
\varphi=\varphi^{\prime}
, and in this case Theorem
6.6
follows from Theorem

6.2
.
If
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{X}
are related by an M2 flop, then we have
\varphi^{\prime}-\varphi=\varphi_{E}[C]
by Lemma
6.5
, and so in this case Theorem
6.6
follows from Theorem

6.4
.
∎

6.3.
The finiteness property

In this section, we prove a finiteness result for the punctured Gromov–Witten invariants
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})
, which implies that the mirror algebra of a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
can be defined over
\mathbb{C}[NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})]
, and not just over the Artinian rings
\mathbb{C}[NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})]/I
. We first introduce the notion of a good divisor in Definition
6.8
and prove the finiteness result under the assumption of existence of a good divisor in Theorem
6.11
.
Then, we show in Lemmas
6.12
-
6.13
that a good divisor always exist after flops, and we deduce from this the general finiteness result in Theorem
6.16
.

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration, with tropicalization
(C(P),C(\mathscr{P}))
.
Let

F=\sum_{p\in P_{\mathbb{Z}}}a_{p}D_{p}

be a divisor on
\mathcal{X}
supported on
\mathcal{X}_{0}
, with
a_{p}\in\mathbb{Z}
.
We denote by
\varphi_{F}:C(P)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
the unique function on
C(P)
which is linear on each cone of
C(\mathscr{P})
and such that
\varphi_{F}(p)=a_{p}
for all
p\in P_{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Lemma 6.7
.

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration, with tropicalization
(C(P),C(\mathscr{P}))
.
For every divisor
F
on
\mathcal{X}
supported on
\mathcal{X}_{0}
, and for every
p,q,r\in C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}
and
\beta\in NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
such that
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})\neq 0
, we have

(6.2)

\varphi_{F}(p)+\varphi_{F}(q)-\varphi_{F}(r)=F\cdot\beta\,.

Proof.

See
[
48
, Corollary 1.14]
.
∎

We define below a notion of good divisor.
Applying Lemma
6.7
to a good divisor
F
will allow us to constrain the curve classes
\beta
such that
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})\neq 0
.

Definition 6.8
.

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration. An effective divisor
F
on
\mathcal{X}
is said to be
good
if
F
is supported on
\mathcal{X}_{0}
and satisfies the following conditions.

i)

The divisor
-F
is nef, and for any compact irreducible curve
C\subset\mathcal{X}_{0}
we have
-F\cdot C\geq 1
, except for finitely many disjoint curves
C
which are either internal exceptional curves in an irreducible component of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
, or irreducible components
D_{ij}=X^{i}\cap X^{j}
of the double locus of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
, satisfying
D_{ij}|_{X^{i}}^{2}=D_{ij}|_{X^{j}}^{2}=-1
.

ii)

The map
f:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{X}}
factors as
\mathcal{X}\xrightarrow{h}\mathcal{Z}\xrightarrow{f^{\prime}}\overline{\mathcal{X}}
, where
h
contracts the finitely many curves
C
in
\mathcal{X}
with
C\cdot F=0
. Moreover, the divisor
h_{\star}F
is Cartier and
-h_{\star}F
is
f^{\prime}
-ample.

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration and
F
a good effective divisor on
\mathcal{X}
.
By Definition
6.8
ii) of a good divisor, the curves
C
with
F\cdot C=0

are exactly the curves contracted by a birational morphism, and so they span a face
G
of the cone
NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})\otimes\mathbb{R}
.
We denote by
J\subset NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
the corresponding monoid ideal consisting of curve classes
\beta\in NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
such that
\beta\notin G
.
By Definition
6.8
i) of a good divisor, the curve classes
\beta\in J
are exactly the curve classes
\beta\in NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
such that
-F\cdot\beta\geq 1
.

As reviewed in §
5.3
, the structure constants
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})
can be computed in terms of the canonical scattering diagrams
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{X}}
, which consists of walls
(W,f_{W})
as in Equation (
5.6
).
In the following two lemmas, we show that the existence of a good effective divisor strongly constrains the canonical scattering diagram
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{X}}
.

Lemma 6.9
.

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta

be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration,
and let
F

be a good effective divisor on
\mathcal{X}
.
Let
(W,f_{W})
be a wall of the canonical scattering diagram
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{X}}
with
f_{W}\neq 1\mod J
. Then,
W
coincides with an edge
e
of
\mathscr{P}
and

(6.3)

f_{W}=\prod_{i\in I_{v,e}}(1+t^{[E_{i}]}z^{m_{v,e}})\prod_{j\in I_{v^{\prime},e}}(1+t^{[E_{j}]}z^{m_{v^{\prime},e}})\mod J\,,

where
v
and
v^{\prime}
are the vertices adjacent to
e
,
(E_{i})_{i\in I_{v,e}}

(resp.
(E_{j})_{j\in I_{v^{\prime},e}}
) is the set of
internal exceptional curves
E_{i}
in
X^{v}
(resp .
X^{v^{\prime}}
) intersecting
X^{e}
, and where
m_{v,e}
(resp.) is the integral primitive direction of
e
pointing towards
v
(resp .
v^{\prime}
).

Proof.

We have to consider
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-curves
C
of class
[C]\notin J
, that is,
[C]\in G
.
As
C
is connected, and the curves in Definition
6.8
i) spanning
G
are disjoint and rigid, it follows that either
C
is a multiple of an internal exceptional curve or a multiple of a double curve.
The formal neighborhood of a double curve is toric, whereas the tropicalization of an
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-curve has a unique leg. Hence, it follows by the toric balancing condition for punctured curves that
C
cannot be a multiple of a double curve.
Thus,
C
is a multiple of an internal exceptional
curve
E_{i}
of an irreducible component

X^{v}
of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
, and intersecting a component
X^{e}\subset X^{v}
of the double locus of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
.
Denote by
f_{W,E_{i}}
the contribution to
f_{W}
of the multiple covers of
E_{i}
.
The formal neighborhood of
E_{i}
in the log scheme
\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\dagger}
is isomorphic to the formal neighborhood of the exceptional curves appearing in the proof of
[
18
, Lemma 4.21]
, and so it follows from
[
18
, Lemma 4.21]
that we have

f_{W,E_{i}}=1+t^{[E_{i}]}z^{m_{v,e}}\,,

where
m_{v,e}
is the integral primitive direction of
e
pointing towards
v
.
This concludes the proof of Lemma
6.9
.
∎

Lemma 6.10
.

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta

be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration,
and let
F

be a good effective divisor on
\mathcal{X}
.
For every
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, there exists finitely many walls
(W,f_{W})
in the canonical scattering diagram
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{X}}
with
f_{W}\neq 1\mod J^{k}
. Moreover, for every such a wall,
f_{W}\mod J^{k}
is a polynomial.

Proof.

We prove the result by induction on
k
.
For
k=0
, the result follows from Lemma
6.9
. For the induction step, let us assume that the result holds for
k-1
, and show that it holds for
k
. For this, first note that every wall
(W,f_{W})
of
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{X}}
with
f_{W}\neq 1\mod J^{k}

and
f_{W}=1\mod J^{k-1}
has an initial point
x\in P
. By consistency of
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{X}}
, either
x\in P_{\mathbb{Z}}
, or
x
is an intersection point of walls which are non-trivial modulo
J^{k-1}
. By the induction hypothesis, there are finitely many walls non-trivial modulo
J^{k-1}
, and so finitely many possible points
x
which are initial points for a wall non-trivial modulo
J^{k}
. Hence, it is enough to prove that for such a point
x
, there are finitely many walls
(W,f_{W})
with initial point
x
such that
f_{W}\neq 1\mod J^{k}
, and that for every such a wall,
f_{W}\mod J^{k}
is a polynomial.

If
x
is contained in the interior of a 2-dimensional face of
\mathscr{P}
, then
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{X}}
is given locally around
x
by a consistent scattering diagram in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Moreover, all the walls passing through
x
are trivial modulo
J
by Lemma
6.9
, and so the walls non-trivial modulo
J^{k}
are uniquely determined by consistency from the walls non-trivial modulo
J^{k-1}
, and so the finiteness and polynomiality of the walls non-trivial modulo
J^{k}
follows from the induction hypothesis and
[
39
, Theorem 6.38]
.

If
x
is contained in the interior of an edge of
\mathscr{P}
, then
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{X}}
is still given locally around
x
by a consistent scattering diagram in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. The only difference with the previous case is that some walls passing through
x
might be trivial modulo
J
.
However, these walls are of the explicit form given by Lemma
6.9
, and so the result still holds by
[
39
, Proposition 6.47]
.

It remains to consider the case where
x=v
is a vertex of
\mathscr{P}
, corresponding to an irreducible component
X^{v}
of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
.
It follows from
[
40
, Theorem 8.15]
that
the punctured Gromov–Witten invariants contributing to the walls
(W,f_{W})
with initial point
x=v
and non-trivial modulo
J^{k}
can be expressed explicitly in terms of punctured Gromov–Witten invariants
N_{\tau}^{\beta}
of
(X^{v},\partial X_{v})
and of the walls non-trivial modulo
J^{k-1}
passing through
v
.
More precisely, it follows from
[
40
, Lemma 8.9]
that, up to applying corner blow-ups to
(X^{v},\partial X^{v})
, the invariants
N_{\tau}^{\beta}
are counts of curves
C
in
X^{v}
intersecting
\partial X^{v}
at fixed smooth points
x_{1},\dots,x_{q}
, with multiplicities
m_{1},\dots,m_{q}
, and at a smooth non-fixed point
x_{\mathrm{out}}
with multiplicity
m_{\mathrm{out}}
.
If
q\neq 1
, then, as in the proof of
[
46
, Lemma 4.1]
, given the points
x_{1}
,…,
x_{q}
, there are only finitely many possibilities for the point
x_{\mathrm{out}}
, depending in a non-trivial way on
x_{1}
,…,
x_{q}
. In particular, for generic
x_{1},\dots,x_{q}
, none of the intersection points of
C
with
\partial X^{v}
are intersection of an internal exceptional curve with
\partial X^{v}
, and so
C
does not contain any internal exceptional curve. If
q=0
and
x_{\mathrm{out}}
is an intersection point of an internal exceptional curve
E
with
\partial X^{v}
, then
\mathcal{O}(C-kE)
has trivial restriction to
\partial X^{v}
. Since the invariant
N_{\tau}^{\beta}
is invariant under deformation of
(X^{v},\partial X^{v})
, it follows from
[
42
, Proposition 4.1]
that
C=kE
if
N_{\tau}^{\beta}\neq 0
.

In all cases, we conclude that, if
N_{\tau}^{\beta}\neq 0
, then either
\beta
is a multiple of an internal exceptional curve, or
\beta
is represented by a curve in
X^{v}
containing no internal exceptional curve and no component of
\partial X^{v}
. In the first case, the corresponding walls are given as in Lemma
6.9
, and so the finiteness and polynomiality is clear. In the second case, using the notations of Definition
6.8
, the class
\beta
is uniquely determined by
h_{\star}\beta
, and
-F\cdot\beta=-h_{\star}F\cdot h_{\star}\beta
. For a wall non-trivial modulo
J^{k}
, we have
-F\cdot\beta\geq k
, and so
-h_{\star}F\cdot h_{\star}\beta\leq k
. Since
-h_{\star}F
is
f^{\prime}
-ample by Definition
6.8
of a good divisor, there are finitely many such classes
\beta
. Combined with the induction hypothesis, this implies the finiteness and polynomiality of the walls non-trivial modulo
J^{k}
.
∎

Theorem 6.11
.

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta

be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration,
with tropicalization
(C(P),C(\mathscr{P}))
,
and let
F

be a good effective divisor on
\mathcal{X}
.
Then, for every
p,q,r\in C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}
, there exists finitely many
\beta\in NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
such that
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})\neq 0
.

Proof.

First note that, by Lemma
6.7
, if
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})\neq 0
, then
-F\cdot\beta
is fixed to be an integer
k
depending only on
p,q,r
. As reviewed in
§
5.3
,
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})
can be computed in terms of broken lines in
C(P)
, or equivalently in terms of jagged paths in
P
. Moreover, these broken lines have fixed asymptotic directions determined by
p
and
q
, and fixed final direction determined by
r
, and so the corresponding jagged paths have fixed initial points, given by the radial projections of
p
and
q
on
P
, and fixed endpoint, given by the radial projection of
r
on
P
.

Let
\mathscr{P}^{\prime}
be the coarsening of
\mathscr{P}
obtained by removing the edges
e
such that the corresponding double curve
X^{e}
of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
is contained in
G
. Such curves are distinct, and so
\mathscr{P}^{\prime}
is just obtained by removing diagonals of some quadrilaterals of
\mathscr{P}
.
By Equation (
5.7
), each time a jagged path crosses an edge of
\mathscr{P}^{\prime}
, the corresponding kink is non-trivial modulo
J
, and so the intersection with
-F
of the curve class attached to the jagged path increases by
1
. Since
-F\cdot\beta=k
is fixed by
p,q,r
, one deduces that the number of times a jagged path contributing to
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})
crosses an edge of
\mathscr{P}^{\prime}
is uniformly bounded. Moreover, if
(W,f_{W})
is a wall generically contained in a 2-dimensional face of
\mathscr{P}^{\prime}
, then
f_{W}=1\mod J
by Lemma
6.9
, and so, by Equation (
5.7
), the number of times a jagged path crosses such a wall is also uniformly bounded. Hence, we have in fact a uniform bound on the number of times a jagged path can cross a wall. In addition, by Lemma
6.9
, there are only finitely many walls non-trivial modulo
J^{k}
, and they all contain only finitely many terms modulo
J^{k}
. Since the jagged paths have fixed asymptotic monomials
z^{p}
or
z^{q}
, we obtain at every wall-crossing finitely many monomials, and since the number of wall-crossings is uniformly bounded, we conclude that there are finitely many possible jagged paths contributing to structures constants of the form
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})
with fixed
p,q,r
, and so in particular finitely
\beta
with
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})\neq 0
.
∎

We will prove the existence of good effective divisors using the following general result in birational geometry.

Lemma 6.12
.

Let
\overline{\mathcal{X}}
be a quasi-projective variety with canonical singularities.
Then, there exists
\mathcal{X}\xrightarrow{g}\mathcal{Z}\xrightarrow{h}\overline{\mathcal{X}}
, where
g
and
h
are projective birational morphisms,
such that the following conditions hold.

i)

\mathcal{X}
has
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial terminal singularities.

ii)

f:=g\circ h:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{X}}
is crepant.

iii)

g:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}
is an isomorphism in codimension one.

iv)

There exists an effective
f
-nef Cartier divisor
F
on
\mathcal{X}
, supported on the exceptional locus of
f
, such that
g_{\star}F
is a Cartier divisor
on
\mathcal{Z}
and
-g_{\star}F
is
h
-ample.

Proof.

First recall that by general MMP,
[
23
, Corollary 1.4.3]
– see also

[
59
, Corollary 2.6]
,
there exists a crepant
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial terminalization of
\overline{\mathcal{X}}
. Moreover,
two such crepant
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial terminalizations are isomorphic in codimension one – see e.g.
[
61
]
, and so have the same number
e(\overline{\mathcal{X}})
of exceptional divisors.

We prove the result by induction on
e(\overline{\mathcal{X}})
. If
e(\overline{\mathcal{X}})=0
, then,
for any crepant
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial terminalization
f:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{X}}
, one can trivially take
\mathcal{Z}=\overline{\mathcal{X}}
,
g=f
,
h=\mathrm{Id}
, and
F=0
.

Assume by induction that the result holds for

e(\overline{\mathcal{X}})<n
with
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
. Let
\overline{\mathcal{X}}
with
e(\overline{\mathcal{X}})=n
.
As
n\geq 1
, by
[
59
, Proposition 2.4]
– see also
[
23
, Corollary 1.4.3]
, there
exists a crepant projective birational morphism
f^{\prime}:\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{X}}
, with
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}

\mathbb{Q}
-factorial, such that
f^{\prime}
has exactly one exceptional divisor
E
, and
-E
is
f^{\prime}
-nef. As
f^{\prime}
is crepant birational, and
-E
is
f^{\prime}
-nef, it follows from the base point free theorem that
-E
is
f^{\prime}
-semi-ample, and so
f^{\prime}
factors as
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{g^{\prime}}\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{h^{\prime}}\overline{\mathcal{X}}
where
g^{\prime}
contracts exactly the irreducible curves
C
with
E\cdot C=0
, and
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}(-E)
is the
g^{\prime}
-pullback of a
h^{\prime}
-ample line bundle on
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
. We claim that
g^{\prime}
is an isomorphism in codimension one: else, it would contract the unique
f^{\prime}
-exceptional divisor
E
, that is,
g_{\star}^{\prime}(-E)=0
, which is effective, whereas
E
is
g^{\prime}
-nef and
-E
is not effective, in contradiction with the negativity lemma
[
67
, Lemma 3.39]
. Hence,
g^{\prime}
is an isomorphism in codimension one,
-g_{\star}^{\prime}E

is Cartier and
h^{\prime}
-ample, and
e(\mathcal{Z}^{\prime})=e(\overline{\mathcal{X}})-1
.

By the induction hypothesis applied to
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
, there exists a crepant
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial terminalization
f^{\prime\prime}=g\circ h^{\prime\prime}:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
, where
g:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}
is an isomorphism in codimension one,
h^{\prime\prime}:\mathcal{Z}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
, and there exists a
f^{\prime\prime}
-nef Cartier divisor
F^{\prime\prime}
on
\mathcal{X}
, supported on the exceptional locus of
f^{\prime\prime}
, such that
g_{\star}F^{\prime\prime}
is a Cartier divisor on
\mathcal{Z}
and
-g_{\star}F^{\prime\prime}
is
h^{\prime\prime}
-ample. To prove the result for
\overline{\mathcal{X}}
, we set
f:=f^{\prime\prime}\circ h^{\prime}:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{X}}
, and
h:=h^{\prime\prime}\circ h^{\prime}:\mathcal{Z}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{X}}
.
As
-g_{\star}^{\prime}E
is
h^{\prime}
-ample and
-g_{\star}F^{\prime\prime}
is
h^{\prime\prime}
-ample, we have that
-g_{\star}F^{\prime\prime}-\lambda g_{\star}^{\prime}E
is
h
-ample for
\lambda
large enough, and it is enough to take
F:=(f^{\prime\prime})^{\star}(-g_{\star}F^{\prime\prime}-\lambda g_{\star}^{\prime}E)
to conclude the proof.

∎

Lemma 6.13
.

Let
\pi\colon\mathcal{X}\to\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration. Then, there exists a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration
\pi^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\rightarrow\Delta
, with affinization
\overline{\mathcal{X}}
and having a good effective divisor.

Proof.

The result follows immediately from Lemma
6.12
, and from the fact that, if
h:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}
is an isomorphism in codimension one, then the exceptional locus of
g
is a configuration of curves as in Definition
6.8
i).
∎

Lemma 6.14
.

Let
\pi\colon\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration, with tropicalization
(C(P),C(\mathscr{P}))
.
Then, for every
p,q,r\in C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}
, there exists finitely many
\beta\in NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
such that
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})\neq 0
.

Proof.

By Lemma
6.13
, there exists a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration

\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\rightarrow\Delta
with affinization
\overline{\mathcal{X}}
, and
with a good effective divisor as in Definition
6.8
.
In particular, it follows by Theorem
6.11
that Lemma
6.14
holds for
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\rightarrow\Delta
.
As
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{X}
are related by a sequence of M1 and M2 flops, Lemma
6.14
holds for
\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
by Theorems

6.2
-
6.4
.
∎

Denote by
h:C(P)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
the height function, given by projection onto the second factor, coming from the definition of
C(P)
as the cone over
P
, so that
P=h^{-1}(1)
.

Lemma 6.15
.

Let
\pi\colon\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration, with tropicalization
(C(P),C(\mathscr{P}))
.
For every
p,q,r\in C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}
and
\beta\in NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
such that
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})\neq 0
, we have
h(r)=h(p)+h(q)
.

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
6.7
applied to
F=\mathcal{X}_{0}
. Indeed, we have
\varphi_{\mathcal{X}_{0}}=h
and
\mathcal{X}_{0}\cdot\beta=0
for all
\beta\in NE(\mathcal{X})
.
∎

Theorem 6.16
.

Let
\pi\colon\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration, with tropicalization
(C(P),C(\mathscr{P}))
.
Then, for every
p,q\in C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}
, there exists finitely many
r\in C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}
and
\beta\in NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
such that
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})\neq 0
.

Proof.

By Lemma
6.15
, for every
p,q,r\in C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}
such that there exists
\beta\in NE(\mathcal{X})
with
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})\neq 0
, we have
h(r)=h(p)+h(q)
.
Given
p,q\in C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}
, the set of integral points
h^{-1}(h(p)+h(q))\cap C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}
is finite, and so it follows that there exists only finitely many
r\in C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}
such that there exists
\beta\in NE(\mathcal{X})
with
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})\neq 0
.
On the other hand, by Lemma
6.14
, for every
p,q,r\in C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}
, there are only finitely many
\beta\in NE(\mathcal{X})
with
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})\neq 0
, and this concludes the proof of Theorem
6.16
.
∎

6.4.
Polarized mirrors and the movable secondary fan

6.4.1.
The polarized mirror family to an open Kulikov degeneration

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration.
It follows from Theorem
6.16
, that the formula (
5.1
) defines an algebra structure on the
\mathbb{C}[NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})]
-module

\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}=\bigoplus_{p\in C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathbb{C}[NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})]\,\vartheta_{p}\,.

Moreover, by Lemma
6.15
,
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}
is a graded algebra for the grading defined by the height function
h:C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. We refer to
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}
as the
mirror algebra
of
\mathcal{X}
.

Definition 6.17
.

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective
open Kulikov degeneration. The
polarized mirror family

(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}},\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{X}})
of
\mathcal{X}
is the family

\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}:=\mathrm{Proj}\,\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}\longrightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}}:=\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})]

endowed with the line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{X}}:=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}}(1)
.

For a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration
\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
, the contraction
\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{X}}
is a relative Mori dream space by Proposition
3.36
.
The corresponding Mori fan
MF(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
is a complete fan in
\mathrm{Pic}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})_{\mathbb{R}}
, whose maximal dimensional cones are indexed by the finitely many
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial birational models
f^{\prime}:\mathcal{X}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{X}^{\prime}

of
\mathcal{X}
over
\overline{\mathcal{X}}
, and are given by

\mathrm{Nef}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})+\langle\mathrm{Ex}(f^{\prime})\rangle
, where
\mathrm{Nef}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
is the relative nef cone of
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
over
\overline{\mathcal{X}}
, and

\langle\mathrm{Ex}(f^{\prime})\rangle
is the non-negative span of the
f^{\prime}
-exceptional divisors.

If
\langle\mathrm{Ex}(f^{\prime})\rangle\neq\{0\}
,
that is, if at least one divisor is contracted, the corresponding cone in
MF(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
is referred to as
bogus
. In other words, a cone is non-bogus if
\mathcal{X}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
is small, that is, by Lemma
6.1
, if
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\rightarrow\Delta

is a projective resolution, and
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\rightarrow\Delta
is an open Kulikov degeneration.
The union of non-bogus cones is the movable cone
\mathrm{Mov}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
.

Following
[
50
]
, we define the
secondary fan

\mathrm{Sec}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
as a coarsening of the Mori fan
MF(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
. We first define the
movable secondary fan

\mathrm{MovSec}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
as a coarsening of the restriction
\mathrm{MovF}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
of the Mori fan to the movable cone
\mathrm{Mov}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
.
Two maximal cones
\mathrm{Nef}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
and
\mathrm{Nef}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
of

\mathrm{MovF}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
are called
equivalent
if the corresponding multivalued PL functions
\varphi^{\prime}
and
\varphi^{\prime\prime}
as in §
6.2
are equal.

Lemma 6.18
.

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration.
There exists a unique fan
\mathrm{MovSec}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
with support the movable cone
\mathrm{Mov}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
, whose maximal cones are the unions of equivalent maximal cones of
\mathrm{MovF}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
.

Proof.

The proof is analogous to the proof of Theorem
[
50
, Theorem 2.12]
.
∎

Lemma 6.19
.

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration. Two maximal cones
\mathrm{Nef}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
and
\mathrm{Nef}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
of
\mathrm{Mov}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
are
equivalent
if and only if
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime}
are connected by a sequence of M1 flops.

Proof.

If
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime}
are connected by a sequence of M1 flops, then
\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\prime\prime}
have the same double curves, so we have
\varphi^{\prime}=\varphi^{\prime\prime}
, and the maximal cones
\mathrm{Nef}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
and
\mathrm{Nef}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
are equivalent.

Conversely, if
\mathrm{Nef}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
and
\mathrm{Nef}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
are equivalent, then, by Lemma
6.18
,
\mathrm{Nef}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
and
\mathrm{Nef}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
are both contained in a maximal cone
C
of
\mathrm{MovSec}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
. As
C
is convex, there exists a path contained in
C
, starting in
\mathrm{Nef}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
, ending
\mathrm{Nef}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
, and only intersecting cones of
MF(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
of codimension zero or one. Therefore, it is enough to prove the result when
\mathrm{Nef}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
and
\mathrm{Nef}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
share a common codimension one face, that is, when
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime}
are related by an elementary flop, which by Lemma
6.1
is either a M1 or a M2 flop. By Lemma
6.5
,
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime}
would not be equivalent if they were related by an M2 flop, and so we conclude that
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime}
are in fact related by an M1 flop.
∎

Let
C
be a maximal cone of
\mathrm{MovSec}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
.
By Theorem
6.6
, all mirror algebras
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}
with
\mathrm{Nef}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})\subset C
have the same structure constants
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime})
. In particular, these structure constants are only non-zero if
\beta\in NE(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
for every
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
with
\mathrm{Nef}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})\subset C
, that is if
\beta\in C^{\vee}_{\mathbb{Z}}
, where
C^{\vee}
is the dual cone of
C
, and so define a
\mathbb{C}[C^{\vee}_{\mathbb{Z}}]
-algebra structure on

\mathcal{R}_{C}:=\bigoplus_{p\in C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathbb{C}[C^{\vee}_{\mathbb{Z}}]\vartheta_{p}\,.

We denote

\mathcal{Y}_{C}:=\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathcal{R}_{C}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[C^{\vee}_{\mathbb{Z}}]\,

and
\mathcal{L}_{C}:=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{Y}_{C}}(1)
.

If
C
and
C^{\prime}
are two maximal cones of
\mathrm{MovSec}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
, with corresponding multivalued PL functions
\varphi_{C}
and
\varphi_{C^{\prime}}
, then it follows from Theorem
6.6
that the map

(6.4)

\displaystyle\psi_{C,C^{\prime}}:\mathcal{R}_{C}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[C^{\vee}_{\mathbb{Z}}]}\mathbb{C}[(C\cap C^{\prime})^{\vee}_{\mathbb{Z}}]

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{R}_{C^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[(C^{\prime})^{\vee}_{\mathbb{Z}}]}\mathbb{C}[(C\cap C^{\prime})^{\vee}_{\mathbb{Z}}]\,,

\displaystyle\vartheta_{p}

\displaystyle\longmapsto t^{\varphi_{C}(p)-\varphi_{C^{\prime}}(p)}\vartheta_{p}\,,

is an isomorphism of
\mathbb{C}[(C\cap C^{\prime})^{\vee}_{\mathbb{Z}}]
-algebras. If
C
,
C^{\prime}
, and
C^{\prime\prime}
are three maximal cones of
\mathrm{MovSec}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
, then the cocycle condition
\psi_{C,C^{\prime\prime}}=\psi_{C,C^{\prime}}\circ\psi_{C^{\prime},C^{\prime\prime}}
holds. Therefore, using the isomorphisms
\psi_{C,C^{\prime}}
, one can glue the families
(\mathcal{Y}_{C},\mathcal{L}_{C})\rightarrow\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[C^{\vee}_{\mathbb{Z}}]

into a polarized family

(6.5)

\nu_{\mathrm{msec}}:(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}},\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}})\longrightarrow S^{\mathrm{\mathrm{msec}}}_{\mathcal{X}}

over the toric variety
S^{\mathrm{\mathrm{msec}}}_{\mathcal{X}}
defined by the movable secondary fan
\mathrm{MovSec}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
.
By Equation (
6.4
),
for every
p\in(hP)_{\mathbb{Z}}
,
\vartheta_{p}
naturally becomes a section of the line bundle
(\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}})^{\otimes h}\otimes\nu_{\mathrm{msec}}^{\star}L_{p}
on
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}}
, where
L_{p}
is the line bundle on
S^{\mathrm{\mathrm{msec}}}_{\mathcal{X}}
defined by the 1-cocycle
t^{\varphi_{C}(p)-\varphi_{C^{\prime}}(p)}
.

Our goal in the remaining part of this section is to describe divisors
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{msec}}_{\mathcal{X}}
, and show that the pair
(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}},\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{msec}}_{\mathcal{X}}+\epsilon\,\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}})
forms a KSBA stable family. We first describe
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}}
below.

Lemma 6.20
.

For every
p\in P_{\mathbb{Z}}
, the line bundle
L_{p}
is trivial, that is,
\vartheta_{p}
is naturally a section of
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
6.5
, we have always
\varphi_{C}(p)-\varphi_{C^{\prime}}(p)=0
for
p\in P_{\mathbb{Z}}
.
∎

By Lemma
6.20
, all the theta functions
\vartheta_{p}
with
p\in P_{\mathbb{Z}}

are sections of
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}}
, and so one can define a divisor on
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}}
as follows.

Definition 6.21
.

Let
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}}
be the family as in Equation (
6.5
) with the ample line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}}
.
We define the
theta divisor
in the linear system of
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}}
as

(6.6)

\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}}:=\left\{\sum_{p\in P_{\mathbb{Z}}}\vartheta_{p}=0\right\}\in|\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}}|\,,

and we denote by
\mathcal{C}_{X}
the restriction of
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}}
to
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}}
.

In what follows we describe the divisor
\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{msec}}_{\mathcal{X}}
in
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}}
.

6.4.2.
The divisor
\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{msec}}_{\mathcal{X}}

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration, with tropicalization
(C(P),C(\mathscr{P}))
. Recall from
§
3.2

that the set
(\partial P)_{\mathbb{Z}}
of integral point in the boundary
\partial P
of
P
is the union of the set
P_{\mathbb{Z}}(0)
of integral points of
P
corresponding to
0
-surfaces and of the set
P_{\mathbb{Z}}(1)
of integral points of
P
corresponding to
1
-surfaces, and that we denote by
(v_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}}
the cyclically ordered integral points in
P_{\mathbb{Z}}(0)
. In particular, we have a decomposition of
\partial P
into intervals:

\partial P=\bigcup_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}}[v_{i},v_{i+1}]\,,

and we have a corresponding decomposition of the boundary
\partial C(P)
of the cone
C(P)
into the cones
C([v_{i},v_{i+1}])
over the intervals
[v_{i},v_{i+1}]
:

\partial C(P)=\bigcup_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}}C([v_{i},v_{i+1}])\,.

For every
v\in P_{\mathbb{Z}}(1)
, the corresponding
0
-surface
X^{v}
has, by Definition

3.4
, a natural map to
\mathbb{A}^{1}
, which is generically a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-fibration, and we denote by
[F_{v}]\in NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
the class of the fibers.
For every
i\in\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}
, denote
v_{i,1},\cdots,v_{i,m_{i}}
the ordered integral points in
P_{\mathbb{Z}}(1)
contained in

[v_{i},v_{i+1}]
, so that we have the interval decomposition
[v_{i},v_{i+1}]=[v_{i},v_{i,1}]\cup\cdots\cup[v_{i,m_{i}},v_{i+1}]
, and the cone decomposition

(6.7)

C([v_{i},v_{i+1}])=C([v_{i},v_{i,1}])\cup\cdots\cup C([v_{i,m_{i}},v_{i+1}])\,.

Let
\varphi_{i}
be a
N_{1}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})\otimes\mathbb{R}
-valued
PL function on
C([v_{i},v_{i+1}])
, linear on each cone of the decomposition (
6.7
), with kink
[F_{v_{i,j}}]
across the ray
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}v_{i,j}
.

Lemma 6.22
.

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration, with tropicalization
(C(P),C(\mathscr{P}))
, and let
r\in\partial C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}
. Then, for every
p,q\in C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}
and
\beta\in NE(\mathcal{X})
, we have either

(i)

N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})=1
if there exists
i\in\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}
such that
p,q,r\in C([v_{i},v_{i+1}])
,
r=p+q
inside
C([v_{i},v_{i+1}])
, and
\beta=\varphi_{i}(p)+\varphi_{i}(q)-\varphi_{i}(r)
, or

(ii)

N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})=0
elsewise.

Proof.

If
p=0
or
q=0
, the result follows from
[
48
, Theorem 4.3]
. Similarly, if
r=0
, the result follows immediately from Definition
5.3
. So, from now on, we assume that
p,q,r
are all non-zero. Denote by
Z_{p}
,
Z_{q}
, and
Z_{r}
the strata of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
corresponding to the smallest cones of
C(P)
containing
p
,
q
, and
r
respectively.
By Definition
5.3
, a punctured map contributing to
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})
has two marked points mapping to
Z_{p}
,
Z_{q}
, and one marked point mapping to a general point of
Z_{r}
. The assumption
r\in(\partial C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}})
implies that
Z_{r}
is either a 0-surface or a 1-surface. But it follows from Definitions
3.2
-
3.4
that there are no compact curve passing through a general point of a 0-surface, and that the only compact curve passing through a general point of a 1-surface is a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-fiber of the
corresponding generically
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-fibration. Hence, we have
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})=0
, unless
p,q,r
are all contained in a common cone
C([v_{i},v_{i+1}])
.

If
p,q,r
are all contained in a common cone
C([v_{i},v_{i+1}])
, the contributing punctured maps have image contained in a chain of
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-fibers of intersecting
1
-surfaces, and so the invariants
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})
are equal to the corresponding invariants for a toric Calabi–Yau 3-fold whose fan contains
C([v_{i},v_{i+1}])
as a boundary face. By
[
48
]
, the invariants
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})
can be computed in terms of broken lines in the canonical scattering diagram. In the toric case, the broken lines are never bending and only gets contributions from the kinks of the PL function
\varphi_{i}
while crossing the rays
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}v_{i,j}
– see
[
16
, §2]
, and so the statement of Lemma
6.22
i) follows.
∎

Remark 6.23
.

Two choices of PL functions
\varphi_{i}
differ by a linear function, and so the curve class
\varphi_{i}(p)+\varphi_{i}(q)-\varphi_{i}(r)
appearing in Lemma
6.22
is independent of the choices.

It follows from Lemma
6.22

that the
\mathbb{C}[NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})]
-submodule

(6.8)

I_{\mathcal{X}}:=\bigoplus_{p\notin\partial C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathbb{C}[NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})]\,\vartheta_{p}\subset\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}

is a graded ideal of
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}
. We denote by

(6.9)

\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}}:=\mathrm{Proj}\,(\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}/I_{\mathcal{X}})\subset\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}=\mathrm{Proj}\,\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}

the corresponding closed subscheme of
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}
over
S_{\mathcal{X}}
.

Lemma 6.24
.

Let
\pi\colon\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration, with tropicalization
(C(P),C(\mathscr{P}))
. Then,
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}}
has toric irreducible components and is a flat family of divisors in the fibers of
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}}
. Moreover, the general fiber of
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}}
is a cycle of smooth rational curves
D_{i,i+1}
in one-to-one correspondence with the intervals
[v_{i},v_{i+1}]\subset\partial P
, and the central fiber
D_{0_{\mathcal{X}}}
over
0_{\mathcal{X}}\in S_{X}
is a cycle of smooth rational curves
D_{v}
in one-to-one correspondence with the boundary integral points
v\in P_{\mathbb{Z}}
. In addition, the theta functions
\vartheta_{p}
with
p\in\partial C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}
naturally restrict to toric monomials on the irreducible components of
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}}
.

Proof.

It follows from Lemma
6.22
that the irreducible components of

\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}}=\mathrm{Proj}\,\bigoplus_{p\in\partial C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}}NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})\,\vartheta_{p}

are

\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X},i,i+1}=\mathrm{Proj}\,\bigoplus_{p\in\partial C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}\cap[v_{i},v_{i+1}]}NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})\,\vartheta_{p}\,,

for
i\in\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}
, and that
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X},i,i+1}\rightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}}
is the Mumford toric degeneration defined by the polyhedral decomposition of
C([v_{i},v_{i+1}])
given in Equation
(
6.7
), and the PL function
\varphi_{i}
. In particular, the general fiber of
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X},i,i+1}\rightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}}
is
\mathbb{P}^{1}
, and its central fiber over
0_{\mathcal{X}}
is a chain of
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-components indexed by the integral points
v\in[v_{i},v_{i+1}]\cap P_{\mathbb{Z}}
. Hence the result follows.
∎

Lemma 6.25
.

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration. Then, the divisors
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}
in the mirror families
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}\rightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}
corresponding to the projective crepant resolutions
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{X}}
glue into a divisor
\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{msec}}_{\mathcal{X}}
in the glued mirror family
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}}\rightarrow S^{\mathrm{\mathrm{msec}}}_{\mathcal{X}}
over the movable secondary fan.

Proof.

This is immediate by the gluing of theta functions in Equation (
6.4
).
∎

6.5.
Torus action and extension over the bogus cones

Let
(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}},\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}})\rightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}}
be the polarized family as in Equation (
6.5
),
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}}
the theta divisor as in Definition
6.21
, and
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{sec}}
the divisor as in Lemma
6.25
.
In this section, we describe how to canonically extend the family
(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}},\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{msec}}_{\mathcal{X}}+\epsilon\,\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}})
defined over
S_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}}
, to a family over the toric stack with stacky fan given by the stacky “secondary fan” defined as follows.

First, recall that the “bogus cones” of the Mori fan
MF(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
, as discussed in §
6.4
, are of the form
\mathrm{Nef}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})+\langle\mathrm{Ex}(f^{\prime})\rangle
, for a birational map
f^{\prime}:\mathcal{X}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
, where
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial. We call two bogus cones
\mathrm{Nef}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})+\langle\mathrm{Ex}(f^{\prime})\rangle
and
\mathrm{Nef}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})+\langle\mathrm{Ex}(f^{\prime\prime})\rangle

equivalent
if the smallest cone of
\mathrm{MovSec}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
containing
\mathrm{Nef}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime})
equals the smallest cone of
\mathrm{MovSec}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
containing
\mathrm{Nef}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime})
.

Definition 6.26
.

The
secondary fan
of
\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}
, denoted by
\mathrm{Sec}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
is the complete fan in
\mathrm{Pic}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})_{\mathbb{R}}
, obtained by adding to the movable secondary fan
\mathrm{MovSec}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
the maximal cones given by the unions of equivalent bogus cones of the Mori fan
MF(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
.

For every bogus cone
\sigma=\mathrm{Nef}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})+\langle\mathrm{Ex}(f^{\prime})\rangle
, let
M_{\sigma}
be the sublattice of
\mathrm{Span}(\sigma)\cap\mathrm{Pic}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})

spanned by
\mathrm{Nef}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})\cap\mathrm{Pic}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
and by the classes of irreducible components of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
contracted by
\mathcal{X}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
. Equivalent bogus cones define the same sublattices, and so we have a well-defined sublattice
M_{\sigma}
for every cone
\sigma
of the secondary fan. The data of the sublattices
M_{\sigma}
is a toric stacky data as in

[
94
, Definition 4.1]
, and so
defines a stacky fan
\mathcal{S}ec(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
with underlying fan
\mathrm{Sec}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
.

We denote from now on by
S^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}}
the proper toric variety whose fan is the secondary fan
\mathrm{Sec}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
, and by
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}}
the proper toric Deligne–Mumford stack with stacky fan
\mathcal{S}ec(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
– see
[
94
, §4.1]
and
[
38
]
for the general theory of stacky fans and toric stacks.
In what follows, we show that the family
(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}},\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{msec}}_{\mathcal{X}}+\epsilon\,\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}})
extends to a family
(\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}},\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}}+\epsilon\,\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}})
over
\mathcal{S}^{sec}_{\mathcal{X}}
. To do this, we first describe a natural torus action on
(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}},\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{msec}}_{\mathcal{X}}+\epsilon\,\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}})
.
Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration. Denote by
T_{0}
the torus
\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{Z}^{P_{\mathbb{Z}}}]
. Note that
\mathbb{Z}^{P_{\mathbb{Z}}}
can be naturally identified with the group of divisors of
\mathcal{X}
supported on
\mathcal{X}_{0}
, since the points of
P_{\mathbb{Z}}
are in a natural one-to-one correspondence with the irreducible components of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
. In particular, for every divisor
F
supported on
\mathcal{X}_{0}
, we have a corresponding one-parameter subgroup
\mathbb{C}^{\star}\subset T_{0}
. There is a natural action of
T_{0}
on
S_{\mathcal{X}}=\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})]
such that, for every divisor
F
supported on
\mathcal{X}_{0}
, the corresponding one-parameter subgroup acts with weights
F\cdot\beta
on the monomials
t^{\beta}
.

Lemma 6.27
.

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration. There exists a unique action of the torus
T_{0}
on the mirror family

\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}=\mathrm{Proj}\,\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}\longrightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}}=\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})]\,,

lifting the natural action on
S_{\mathcal{X}}
, such that for every divisor
F
on
\mathcal{X}
supported on
\mathcal{X}_{0}
, the corresponding one-parameter subgroup acts on the monomial
t^{\beta}\vartheta_{p}
with weight
\varphi_{F}(p)+F\cdot\beta
, where
\varphi_{F}
is as in §
6.3
.

Proof.

This follows immediately from Lemma
6.7
– see also
[
48
, Construction 1.24]
in the general context of the intrinsic mirror construction.
∎

The action of the torus
T_{0}
on the affine toric varieties
S_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}
corresponding to the projective crepant resolutions
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{X}}
naturally induces an action of
T_{0}
on the toric varieties
S^{\mathrm{\mathrm{msec}}}_{\mathcal{X}}
and
S^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}}
, and on the toric stack
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}}
. It follows from the Equation (
6.4
) that the actions of
T_{0}
on the families
