\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}\to S_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}
given by Lemma
6.27
glue into an action of
T_{0}
on
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}}\rightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}}
.

Theorem 6.28
.

There exists a unique
T_{0}
equivariant extension of the family
(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}},\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{msec}}_{\mathcal{X}}+\epsilon\,\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}})\rightarrow S^{\mathrm{\mathrm{msec}}}_{\mathcal{X}}
to a family
(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{sec}},\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}}+\epsilon\,\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}})\to\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}}
.

Proof.

This follows by the existence of the action of
T_{0}
on
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}}\to S_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{\mathrm{msec}}}
and
[
50
, Corollary 5.7]
.
∎

Remark 6.29
.

By Theorem
6.28
, the line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}}
on
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}}
extends to a line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{sec}}
on
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{sec}}
. Moreover, by Lemma
6.27
, the theta functions
\vartheta_{p}
with
p\in P_{\mathbb{Z}}
extend to sections of
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{sec}}
.
By Definition
6.26
, every cone of the secondary fan
\mathrm{Mov}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
is of the form
\rho+\sum_{v\in I}[X^{v}]
, where
\rho
is a cone of
\mathrm{MovSec}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
and
I
is a set of integral points
v\in P_{\mathbb{Z}}
such that the corresponding irreducible components
X^{v}
of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
are either 1-surfaces or 2-surfaces.
By
[
50
, §6.4]
, in restriction to the toric stratum of
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}}
corresponding to the cone
\rho+\sum_{v\in I}\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}[X^{v}]
of
\mathrm{Sec}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
, the divisor
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}}
given by Theorem
6.28
is given by the equation
\sum_{p\in P_{\mathbb{Z}}\setminus I}\vartheta_{p}=0
.

7.
KSBA stability of the mirror family to an open Kulikov degeneration

In this section we first prove the finite generation of their mirror algebras to open Kulikov degenerations, in Theorem
7.4
. Then, in Theorem
7.7
we prove that the restriction of the mirror family to the dense torus in the base is KSBA stable. Finally, in Theorem
7.13
we prove the KSBA stability over the full base.

7.1.
Finite generation

We show in Lemma
7.2
that the polarized mirror family to an open Kulikov degeneration restricts to a Mumford toric degeneration over a particular stratum of the base. This will be used in the following sections to prove finite generation.

7.1.1.
Central fibers of polarized mirror families

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
\pi\colon\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration. The central fiber
(Y_{0_{\mathcal{X}}},L_{0,\mathcal{X}})
of the polarized mirror family

\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}}
over the unique torus fixed point
0_{\mathcal{X}}
of the affine toric variety
S_{\mathcal{X}}=\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})]
is the union of toric surfaces with intersection complex
(P,\mathscr{P})
.
In particular, the set of irreducible components of
Y_{0_{\mathcal{X}}}
is in one-to-one correspondence with
P_{\mathbb{Z}}
, all the irreducible components of
Y_{0_{\mathcal{X}}}
are isomorphic to
\mathbb{P}^{2}
, and the line bundle
L_{0,\mathcal{X}}
restricted to each irreducible component of
Y_{0_{\mathcal{X}}}
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(1)
.

Proof.

This follows from
[
45
, §2.1]
, where the central fibers of mirror families given by the intrinsic mirror construction are described explicitly.
∎

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\to\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration and let
F
be a good effective divisor on
\mathcal{X}
. Let
G
be the face of any
\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}
spanned by the finitely many curves
C
such that
F\cdot C=0
, and
J=NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})\setminus G
. Then, the restriction of the family
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}\to\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{X}}
to the toric stratum

\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[G]\cong\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})/J]\subset S_{\mathcal{X}}

is a Mumford toric degeneration with central fiber
Y_{0_{\mathcal{X}}}
.

Proof.

As shown in §
6.3
, the wall-crossing transformations induced by the walls of the canonical scattering diagram are trivial modulo
J
, except for the contributions of the kinks corresponding to the finitely double curves
C
of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
with
F\cdot C=0
. The results follows by
[
41
, §2.1]
– see also
[
39
, §6.2.1]
and
[
16
, §2]
.
∎

7.1.2.
Proof of finite generation

For every face
H
of
NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
, the affine toric variety
\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[H]

is a toric stratum of the affine toric variety
\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})]
.
We denote by
T_{H}:=\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[H^{\mathrm{gp}}]
the corresponding open dense torus in

\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[H]
.

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration. Then, for every face
H
of
NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
, the restriction
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}|_{T_{H}}\rightarrow T_{H}
of the mirror family to
T_{H}
is a finite type morphism, that is,
R_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[H^{\mathrm{gp}}]
is a finitely generated
\mathbb{C}[H^{\mathrm{gp}}]
-algebra.

Proof.

As
f:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{X}}
is a relative Mori dream space by Proposition
3.36
, for every face
H
of
NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
, there is a corresponding birational contraction

f_{H}:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{H}
such that
H=NE(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{X}_{H})
.
By Equation (
6.4
), the restriction
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}|_{T_{H}}\rightarrow T_{H}
of the mirror family to
T_{H}
is independent of the choice of the particular choice of crepant resolution
\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{H}
. By Lemma
6.12
, there exists a crepant resolution
\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{H}
such that there exists a good divisor
F
on
\mathcal{X}
over
\mathcal{X}_{H}
. To prove Lemma
7.3
, we will show that for such a particular crepant resolution,
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[H]
is a finitely generated
\mathbb{C}[H]
-algebra, which implies in particular that
R_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[H^{\mathrm{gp}}]
is a finitely generated
\mathbb{C}[H^{\mathrm{gp}}]
-algebra.

Let
G
be the face of
H
spanned by the finitely many curves
C
such that
F\cdot C=0
, and denote
by
J:=H\setminus G
the corresponding monoid ideal. By Lemma
7.2
,
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[H]/J
is a Stanley-Reisner algebra over
\mathbb{C}[H]/J\simeq\mathbb{C}[G]
, and so in particular a finitely generated
\mathbb{C}[H]/J
-algebra. In particular, there exists a finite set
\{\vartheta_{p}\}_{p\in I}
of theta functions generating
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[H]/J
as
\mathbb{C}[H]/J
-algebra. We will show that

\{\vartheta_{p}\}_{p\in I}
actually generate
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[H]
as
\mathbb{C}[H]
-algebra. Denoting by
(\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[H])^{\prime}
the
\mathbb{C}[H]
-subalgebra of
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[H]
generated by
\{\vartheta_{p}\}_{p\in I}
, we have to show that
(\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[H])^{\prime}=\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[H]
. To do this, we follow the path of the proof of
[
41
, Proposition 6.6]

(see also
[
64
, Lemma 16.6]
and
[
71
, Proposition 4.19]
).

By Lemma
6.27
,
the divisor
-F
induces an action of
\mathbb{C}^{\star}
on
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[H]
such that the monomials
t^{\beta}\vartheta_{p}
with
\beta\in H
and
p\in C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}
are eigenvectors with weight
-\varphi_{F}(p)-F\cdot\beta\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. For every
w,h\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, denote by
V_{w,h}
the set of monomials

t^{\beta}\vartheta_{p}
of weight
w
with
p
of height
h
, that is, with
p\in(hP)_{\mathbb{Z}}
. As the monomials
t^{\beta}\vartheta_{p}
are a linear basis of
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[H]
, it is enough to show that, for every
w,h\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, we have
V_{w,h}\subset(\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[H])^{\prime}
.

Fix
w,h\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. We prove that every monomial

t^{\beta}\vartheta_{p}
in
V_{v,w}
is actually in

(\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[H])^{\prime}
by decreasing induction on
-F\cdot\beta
. First note that, as
(hP)_{\mathbb{Z}}
is finite, there are finitely many possible values for
-\varphi_{F}(p)
, and so, as
w=-\varphi_{F}(p)-F\cdot\beta
is fixed, there are finitely many possible values for
-F\cdot\beta
for
t^{\beta}\vartheta_{p}
in
V_{v,w}
. Hence, the result is immediate for large enough values of
-F\cdot\beta
.
For the induction step, as
\{\vartheta_{q}\}_{q\in C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}}
generates
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[H]/J
, one can write
\vartheta_{p}=a+b
, with
a\in(\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[H])^{\prime}
and
b\in J(\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[H])
, and with both
a
and
b
homogeneous with respect to the
\mathbb{C}^{\star}
-action and of height
h
. Hence, we have
t^{\beta}\vartheta_{p}=t^{\beta}a+t^{\beta}b
, with
t^{\beta}a\in(\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[H])^{\prime}
, and
t^{\beta}b\in V_{w,h}
.
Actually, as
b\in J(\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[H])
, for every monomial
t^{\beta^{\prime}}\vartheta_{q}
in
b
, we have
-F\cdot\beta^{\prime}\geq 1
. Hence, every monomial in
t^{\beta}b
is of the form
t^{\beta+\beta^{\prime}}\vartheta_{q}
with
-F\cdot(\beta+\beta^{\prime})>-F\cdot\beta
. It follows from the induction hypothesis that
t^{\beta}b\in(\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[H])^{\prime}
, and so finally that
t^{\beta}\vartheta_{p}\in(\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[H])^{\prime}
.
∎

Theorem 7.4
.

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration. Then, the mirror algebra

\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}
is a finitely generated
\mathbb{C}[NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})]
-algebra.

Proof.

As there are finitely many faces of
NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
, it follows from Lemma
7.3
that there exists a finite set
\{\vartheta_{p}\}_{p\in I}
such that, for every face
H
of
NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
,
\{\vartheta_{p}\}_{p\in I}
generate
R_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[H^{\mathrm{gp}}]
as a
\mathbb{C}[H^{\mathrm{gp}}]
-algebra. Denote by
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}
the subalgebra of
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}
generated by
\{\vartheta_{p}\}_{p\in I}
. Both
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}
and
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}
are graded by the height function
h:C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. Hence, denoting for every
h\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
by
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X},h}
and
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X},h}^{\prime}
the subspaces of
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}
and
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}
of degree
h
, we have
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X},h}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X},h}
.
As
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X},h}
is a free finite rank
\mathbb{C}[NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})]
-module, with basis
\{\vartheta_{p}\}_{p\in(hP)_{\mathbb{Z}}}
, it follows that the quotient
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X},h}/\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X},h}^{\prime}
is a finitely generated
\mathbb{C}[NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})]
-module.
On the other hand, for every face
H
of
NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
, we have
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X},h}/\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X},h}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbb{C}[H^{\mathrm{gp}}]=0
. As the tori
\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[H^{\mathrm{gp}}]
cover
\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})]
, it follows from Nakayama’s lemma that
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X},h}/\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X},h}^{\prime}=0
. Hence,
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X},h}=\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X},h}^{\prime}
for all
h
, and so
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}=\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}
is finitely generated.
∎

7.2.
Generic KSBA stability of the mirror family

In this section, we prove in Theorem
7.7
the KSBA stability of the restriction of the extended mirror family
(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{sec}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{sec}}+\epsilon\,\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{sec}})\rightarrow\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{sec}}
to the dense torus in

\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{sec}}
.

Theorem 7.5
.

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a
quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration. Then, the mirror family

(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\rightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}}

is a projective flat family of semi-log-canonical surfaces
(Y_{t},D_{t})
such that
K_{Y_{t}}+D_{t}=~0
.

Proof.

First note that
(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\rightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}}
is flat since the algebra
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}
of theta functions is a free
NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
-module. On the other hand, by Theorem
7.4
, the algebra
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}
is finitely generated, and so the morphism
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}=\mathrm{Proj}\,\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}}
is projective.

To show that the fiber
(Y_{t},D_{t})
over
t\in S_{\mathcal{X}}
is semi-log-canonical and
K_{Y_{t}}+D_{t}=0
, consider the smallest toric stratum
\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[H]
of
S_{\mathcal{X}}

containing
t
, corresponding to a face
H
of
NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
. In particular,
t
is contained in the dense torus orbit
T_{H}:=\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[H^{\mathrm{gp}}]
in
\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[H]
.
As in the proof of Lemma
7.3
, there is a corresponding birational contraction

f_{H}:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{H}
such that
H=NE(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{X}_{H})
, and the restriction
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}|_{T_{H}}
is independent of the choice of the crepant resolution
\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{H}
.
Hence, by Lemma
6.12
, we can assume that there exists a good divisor
F
on
\mathcal{X}
over
\mathcal{X}_{H}
. Let
G
be the face of
H
spanned by the curves
C
with
F\cdot C=0
, and denote
J:=H\setminus G
. Consider the one-parameter subgroup
\mathbb{C}^{\star}\subset T_{0}
associated to
F
as in §
6.5
. Consider the corresponding orbit
\mathbb{C}^{\star}\in T_{H}
passing through
t
. Its closure in
\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[H]
is an affine line
\mathbb{A}^{1}
with
0\in\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[G]\simeq\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[H]/J
. By
Lemma
6.27
, the restriction of
(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}})
to this line is a
\mathbb{C}^{\star}
-equivariant family.
Hence, by the proof of
[
43
, Lemma 8.33]
, it is enough to show Theorem
7.5
for the central fiber of this family over
0\in\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[G]\simeq\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[H]/J
. By Lemma
7.2
, this central fiber is a fiber of a Mumford toric degeneration. Hence, the result follows from
[
6
]
.
∎

Theorem 7.6
.

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a
quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration. Then, the extended mirror family

(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{sec}},\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}})\rightarrow\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}}

is a projective flat family of semi-log-canonical surfaces
(Y_{t},D_{t})
such that
K_{Y_{t}}+D_{t}=0
.

Proof.

For the fibers of
(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}},\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{msec}}_{\mathcal{X}})\rightarrow S^{\mathrm{\mathrm{msec}}}_{\mathcal{X}}
, the result follows from Theorem
7.5
applied to the various projective crepant resolutions of
\overline{\mathcal{X}}
. By Theorem
6.28
, any fiber of
(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{sec}},\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}})\rightarrow\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}}
is related by
T_{0}
-action, and so is isomorphic, to a
fiber of
(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{msec}},\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{msec}}_{\mathcal{X}})\rightarrow S^{\mathrm{\mathrm{msec}}}_{\mathcal{X}}
, and so the general result follows.
∎

Theorem 7.7
.

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a
quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration.
For every
t\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}}
, let
(Y_{t},D_{t}+\epsilon\,C_{t})
be the fiber over
t
of the extended mirror family

(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{sec}},\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}}+\epsilon\,\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}})\rightarrow\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}}
. Then, the following holds:

i)

For every
t\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}}
, if the surface
Y_{t}
is normal, then the pair
(Y_{t},D_{t})
is log canonical, and the pair
(Y_{t},D_{t}+\epsilon\,C_{t})
is KSBA stable.

ii)

If
t
is contained in the dense torus orbit
T_{\mathcal{X}}:=\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[N_{1}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})]\subset\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}}
, then the surface
Y_{t}
is normal, the pair
(Y_{t},D_{t})
is log canonical, and the pair
(Y_{t},D_{t}+\epsilon\,C_{t})
is KSBA stable.

Proof.

We first prove i).
By Theorem
7.6
, the pair
(Y_{t},D_{t})
is semi-log-canonical, and so log canonical since the surface
Y_{t}
is assumed to be normal. Hence,
by
[
50
, Proposition 7.2]
, to prove that
(Y_{t},D_{t}+\epsilon\,C_{t})
is semi-log-canonical, it is enough to show that
C_{t}
does not contain any 0-dimensional stratum of
D_{t}
. By Remark
6.29
, the divisor
C_{t}
is defined by an equation of the form
\sum_{p\in P_{\mathbb{Z}}\setminus I}\vartheta_{p}=0
for some subset
I\subset P_{\mathbb{Z}}
consisting of integral points corresponding to either 1-surfaces or 2-surfaces in
\mathcal{X}_{0}
. By Equations (
6.8
)-(
6.9
), we have
\vartheta_{p}|_{D_{t}}=0
if
p\notin\partial P_{\mathbb{Z}}
. On the other hand, by Lemma
6.24
,
D_{t}
is a cycle of rational curves, with
0
-dimensional strata
x_{v}
in one-to-one correspondence with the integral points
v
of
\partial P
corresponding to the 0-surfaces in
\mathcal{X}_{0}
. Moreover, the theta functions
\vartheta_{p}
with
p\in\partial P
restrict to toric monomials on the irreducible components of
D_{t}
. It follows that for every
0
-dimensional stratum
x_{v}
of
D_{t}
, we have
\vartheta_{p}(x_{v})\neq 0
if
p=v
, and
\vartheta_{p}(x_{v})=0
if
p\in P_{\mathbb{Z}}\setminus\{v\}
. Hence, we have
\sum_{p\in P_{v}}\vartheta_{p}(x_{v})\neq 0
, and so
x_{v}\notin C_{t}
. This concludes the proof of i).

To prove ii), it is enough to prove that
Y_{t}
is normal, since then the other conditions follow by i).
By Theorem
7.5
, the pair
(Y_{t},D_{t})
is semi-log-canonical. In particular, the
Y_{t}
is
S_{2}
, and so, by Serre’s criterion for normality
[
51
, Thm 8.22A]
, it suffices to show that
Y_{t}
is regular in codimension one. As in the proofs of Lemma
7.3
and Theorem
7.5
, we can assume that there exists a good divisor
F
on
\mathcal{X}
, inducing a
\mathbb{C}^{\star}
-action on
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}}
. The closure of the
\mathbb{C}^{\star}
-orbit passing through
t
is an affine line
\mathbb{A}^{1}
, with
(\mathbb{A}^{1}\setminus\{0\})\subset T_{\mathcal{X}}
, and
0\in\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[G]\simeq\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[H]/J
, where
G=F^{\perp}\cap NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
and
J=\mathbb{C}[NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})]\setminus J
. The restriction
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}|_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
of
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}
to this affine line is constant with fibers
Y_{t}
on
\mathbb{A}^{1}\setminus\{0\}
. Moreover, by the proof of Lemma
7.2
, the central fiber
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X},0}

of
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}|_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}

is a union of toric varieties with intersection complex
(P,\mathscr{P}_{J})
, where
\mathscr{P}_{J}
is the subdivision of
P
obtained from
\mathscr{P}_{J}
by omitting the edges
e
such that the corresponding curves
C_{e}
in the double locus of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
satisfy
F\cdot C_{e}=0
. In particular, the singular locus of
(P,\mathscr{P}_{J})
is the union of its double curves
D_{e}
, which are in one-to-one correspondence with the edges
e
of
\mathscr{P}_{J}
, that is, with the double curves

C_{e}
of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
such that
F\cdot C_{e}\neq 0
. Hence to prove that
Y_{t}
is smooth in codimension one, it is enough to show that each of the double curves
D_{e}
are generically smoothed out in
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}|_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
.

It follows from the construction of
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}}
from the canonical scattering diagram, together with
[
45
, Eq.(2.17)]
,
that a neighborhood of
D_{e}
in
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}|_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
is isomorphic to the affine variety of equation
xy=t^{[C_{e}]}f_{W,e}
, where
f_{W,e}
is the function of the canonical scattering diagram attached to the wall supported on the edge
e
of
P
.
As
(\mathbb{A}^{1}\setminus\{0\})
is contained in
T_{\mathcal{X}}
, we have
t^{[C_{e}]}\neq 0
on the non-central fibers of
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}|_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
. Moreover, by
Lemma
6.9
, the function
f_{W,e}
is generically non-zero on
D_{e}
. This shows that all the double curves
D_{e}
of
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X},0}
are generically smoothed out in the one-parameter family
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}|_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
, and this concludes the proof that
Y_{t}
is normal.
∎

7.3.
KSBA stability of the mirror family

In this section, we prove the KSBA stability of the mirror family
(\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}},\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}}+\epsilon\,\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}})\rightarrow\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}}
. To do this, we first provide an inductive description of the fibers.

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration, and let
H
be a face of
NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
.
As
f:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{X}}
is a relative Mori dream space by Proposition
3.36
, there is a corresponding birational contraction

f_{H}:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{H}
such that
H=NE(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{X}_{H})
.
Denote by

S_{H}:=\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[H]=\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[NE(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{X}_{H})]
the corresponding stratum of
S_{\mathcal{X}}
, with dense torus orbit
T_{H}:=\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[H^{\mathrm{gp}}]
.
Let
\mathscr{P}_{H}
be the polyhedral decomposition of
P
obtained as a coarsening of
\mathscr{P}
by removing all edges
e
of
\mathscr{P}
such that the corresponding double curves
C_{e}
of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
satisfy
[C_{e}]\in H
, that is, are contracted by
f_{H}
.
For every 2-dimensional cell
\sigma
of
\mathscr{P}_{H}
, we denote by
\mathscr{P}_{\sigma}
the polyhedral decomposition of
\sigma
obtained by restricting
\mathscr{P}
to
\sigma
.

Recall that the 2-dimensional cells
\tau
of
\mathscr{P}
are lattice triangles of size one, in one-to-one correspondence with the triple points
x_{\tau}
of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
. If
\tau
and
\tau^{\prime}
are contained in the same 2-dimensional cell
\sigma
of
\mathscr{P}_{H}
, then the triple points
x_{\tau}
and
x_{\tau^{\prime}}
are connected by a chain of double curves which are all contracted by
f_{H}
, and so we have

f_{H}(\tau)=f_{H}(\tau^{\prime})
. Hence, for every 2-dimensional face
\sigma
of
\mathscr{P}_{H}
, there is a unique point
x_{\sigma}\in\mathcal{X}_{H}
such that
f_{H}(x_{\tau})=x_{\sigma}
for every 2-dimensional cell
\tau
of
\mathscr{P}
contained in
\sigma
.

Lemma 7.8
.

Let
\sigma
be a 2-dimensional cell of
\mathscr{P}_{H}
. Then, the following holds:

i)

For every edge
e\in\mathscr{P}^{[1]}
such that
e\subset\sigma
and
e\not\subset\partial\sigma
, corresponding to the double curve
C_{e}
of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
, we have
f_{H}(C_{e})=\{x_{\sigma}\}
.

ii)

For every vertex
v\in\mathscr{P}^{[0]}
such that
v\in\sigma
and
v\notin\partial\sigma
, corresponding to the irreducible component
X^{v}
of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
, we have
f_{H}(X^{v})=\{x_{\sigma}\}
.

Proof.

First note that i) follows immediately from the definition of
\mathscr{P}_{H}
. For ii), as
v\notin\partial\sigma
, it follows from i) that all the irreducible components of
\partial X^{v}
are contracted by
f_{H}
to
x_{\sigma}
. This implies that
X^{v}
is also contracted by
f_{H}
to
x_{\sigma}
since, by Theorem
3.34
,
X^{v}
is a positive log Calabi–Yau surface and so
\partial X^{v}
supports a big divisor.
∎

It follows from Lemma
7.8
that one can find an affine open subset
U_{\sigma}
of
\mathcal{X}_{H,0}
containing
x_{\sigma}
such that
f_{H}(C_{e})\cap U_{\sigma}=\emptyset
for every edge
e\in\mathscr{P}^{[1]}

such that
e\not\subset\sigma
. We denote

\mathcal{X}_{\sigma,0}:=f_{H}^{-1}(U_{\sigma})\subset\mathcal{X}_{0}\,.

By Lemma
7.8
, the irreducible components
X^{v}_{\sigma}
of
\mathcal{X}_{\sigma,0}
are naturally in one-to-one correspondence with the integral points
v\in\sigma_{\mathbb{Z}}
:

\mathcal{X}_{\sigma,0}=\bigcup_{v\in\sigma_{\mathbb{Z}}}X^{v}_{\sigma}\,.

Lemma 7.9
.

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration and let
H
be a face of
NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
. Then, for every 2-dimensional cell
\sigma
of
\mathscr{P}_{H}
, there exists a quasi-projective open Kulikov surface
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{\sigma,0}
, with dual intersection complex
(\sigma,\mathscr{P}_{\sigma})
, such that

\mathcal{X}_{\sigma,0}
is a Zariski open dense subset of
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{\sigma,0}
, containing all compact curves of
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{\sigma,0}
, and
U_{\sigma}
is a Zariski open neighborhood of the central point in the affinization

\overline{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}_{\sigma,0}
.

Proof.

First note that
\mathcal{X}_{\sigma,0}
is quasi-projective, reduced and normal crossings, as an open subset of the quasi-projective, reduced and normal crossings surface
\mathcal{X}_{0}
.
If
v\in\sigma_{\mathbb{Z}}
and
v\notin\partial\sigma
, then it follows from Lemma
7.8
ii)
that
X^{v}_{\sigma}=X^{v}
, and so
X^{v}_{\sigma}
is a 2-surface. If
v\in\partial\sigma
, then it follows from Definitions
3.2
-
3.4
that
X^{v}_{\sigma}
is naturally a Zariski open subset of a 0-surface or a 1-surface if
\dim f_{H}(X^{v}_{\sigma})=2
or
\dim f_{H}(X^{v}_{\sigma})=1
respectively.
Finally, it follows from
[
86
, Proposition 3.3]

that
\sigma
is homeomorphic to a disk.
∎

Let
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{\sigma,0}
be the quasi-projective open Kulikov surface given by
Lemma
7.9
.
By Theorem
3.21
,
we can assume up to locally trivial deformations that
\mathcal{X}_{\sigma,0}^{\prime}
is generic
d
-semistable, and so that
\mathcal{X}_{\sigma,0}^{\prime}
is the central fiber of a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration
\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}\rightarrow\Delta
by
Theorem
3.26
and
Lemma
3.31
.
The inclusions
\mathcal{X}_{\sigma,0}\subset\mathcal{X}_{0}
and
\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma,0}\subset\mathcal{X}_{H,0}
induce an injective map
NE(\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma})\rightarrow H=NE(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{X}_{H})
, and so a dominant toric morphism

(7.1)

S_{H}=\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[H]\longrightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}}=\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[NE(\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma})]\,.

Lemma 7.10
.

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration, and let
H
be a face of
NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
.
Then, for every
\beta\in H
and
p,q,r\in C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}
, we have
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})=0
unless
p,q,r
are all contained in a common cone
C(\sigma)
over a 2-dimensional cell
\sigma
of
\mathscr{P}_{H}
.

Proof.

The tropicalization of a punctured log curve
C
contributing to
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})
as
in Definition
5.3

is a connected tropical curve in
C(P)
, with two unbounded edges with asymptotic directions
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}p
and
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}q
, and with one leg contained in
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}r
. In particular, if
p,q,r
were not all contained in a common cone
C(\sigma)
, this tropical curve would have to intersect a cone
C(\sigma\cap\sigma^{\prime})
over the intersection of two distinct 2-dimensional faces
\sigma
,
\sigma^{\prime}
of
\mathscr{P}_{\sigma}
, and so a cone
C(e)
over an edge
e
of
\mathscr{P}_{\sigma}
. In particular, the curve
C
would contain an irreducible component with class a multiple of
[C_{e}]
, where
C_{e}
is the double curve of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
corresponding to
e
. Since
e
is an edge of
\mathscr{P}_{\sigma}
, we have
[C_{e}]\notin H
, and this would imply
\beta\notin H
. Hence, the results follows since we assume that
\beta\in H
.
∎

Lemma 7.11
.

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration and let
H
be a face of
NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
. Then,
for every 2-dimensional cell
\sigma
of
\mathscr{P}_{H}
,
for every
p,q,r\in C(\sigma)_{\mathbb{Z}}
and
\beta\in H
, we have
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})=0
if
\beta\notin NE(\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma})
, and

N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})=N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X}_{\sigma})

for every
\beta\in NE(\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma})
.

Proof.

Let
Z_{r}
be the stratum of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
corresponding to the smallest cone of
C(P)
containing
r
. As
r\in C(\sigma)_{\mathbb{Z}}
, it follows from Lemma
7.8
that
x_{\sigma}\in f_{H}(Z_{r})
.
Recall that the Definition
5.3
of
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})
involves considering punctured log curves passing through a given point
z
in the interior of
Z_{r}
. As
U_{\sigma}
is an open neighborhood of
x_{\sigma}
, there exists a point
z
in the interior of
Z_{r}
such that
f_{H}(z)\in U_{\sigma}
. If one uses such a point
z
to calculate
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})
, then all the contributing punctured log curves are contained in
f_{H}^{-1}(z)
and so in
\mathcal{X}_{\sigma,0}
, since
\beta\in H
and so curves of class
\beta
are contracted by
f_{H}
. In particular, we necessarily have
\beta\in NE(\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma})
if
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})\neq 0
.
Finally, as all these curves are contained in
\mathcal{X}_{\sigma,0}
, and the log structure on
\mathcal{X}_{\sigma,0}
is the restriction of the log structure on
\mathcal{X}_{0}
, we have

N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})=N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X}_{\sigma})
by
Lemma
5.6
.
∎

Theorem 7.12
.

Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration and let
H
be a face of
NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
. Then, the following holds:

i)

There is a natural one-to-one correspondence between the irreducible components of
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}|_{S_{H}}
and the 2-dimensional cells
\sigma
of
\mathscr{P}_{H}
.

ii)

For every 2-dimensional cell
\sigma
of
\mathscr{P}_{H}
, denoting by
\mathcal{Y}_{\sigma}
the corresponding irreducible component of
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}|_{S_{H}}
, by

\partial\mathcal{Y}_{\sigma}
,
\mathcal{D}_{\sigma}
and
\mathcal{C}_{\sigma}
the restrictions of
the double locus of
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}|_{S_{H}}
, of

\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}}|_{S_{\mathcal{X}}}

and
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{X}}|_{S_{\mathcal{X}}}

to
\mathcal{Y}_{\sigma}
,
the family
(\mathcal{Y}_{\sigma},\mathcal{D}_{\sigma}+\partial\mathcal{Y}_{\sigma}+\epsilon\,\mathcal{C}_{\sigma})\rightarrow S_{H}

is the base change, along the natural map
S_{H}\rightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}}
as in
Equation (
7.1
),
of the mirror family
(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}}+\epsilon\,\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}})
to the quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration
\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}\rightarrow\Delta
.

Proof.

Denoting
J_{H}:=NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})\setminus H
,
we have
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}|_{S_{H}}=\mathrm{Proj}\,\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}/J_{H}
. Explicitly,
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}/J_{H}
is the algebra of theta functions
(\vartheta_{p})_{p\in C_{\mathbb{Z}}}
where the structure constants
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})
with
\beta\notin H
are set to zero.
By Lemma
7.10
, the structure constants
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})
with
\beta\in H
are zero unless
p,q,r
are contained in a common cone
C(\sigma)
over a 2-dimensional cell
\sigma
of
\mathscr{P}_{H}
. Therefore, for every such
\sigma
, denoting by
\mathcal{R}_{\sigma}

the
\mathbb{C}[H]
-algebra
spanned by the theta functions
(\vartheta_{p})_{p\in C(\sigma)_{\mathbb{Z}}}
, it follows that
\mathcal{Y}_{\sigma}:=\mathrm{Proj}\,\mathcal{R}_{\sigma}
is an union of irreducible components of
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}|_{S_{H}}
.
Moreover, denoting by
\partial\mathcal{Y}_{\sigma}
the union of
\mathcal{Y}_{\sigma}\cap\mathcal{Y}_{\sigma^{\prime}}
with
\sigma^{\prime}\neq\sigma
, it follows that
\partial\mathcal{Y}_{\sigma}
is defined by
\vartheta_{p}=0
for every
p\in C(\sigma)_{\mathbb{Z}}
such that there does not exist
\sigma^{\prime}\neq\sigma
with
p\in C(\sigma^{\prime})
. On the other hand, by the definition of
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}}
in Equations (
6.8
)-(
6.9
), the restriction
\mathcal{D}_{\sigma}:=\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}}|_{\mathcal{Y}_{\sigma}}
is defined by
\vartheta_{p}=0
for
p\notin(C(\partial\sigma)\cap C(\partial P))_{\mathbb{Z}}
. For every
p\in C(\partial\sigma)_{\mathbb{Z}}
, we have
p\in C(\sigma^{\prime})
for some
\sigma^{\prime}\neq\sigma
or
p\in(C(\partial\sigma)\cap C(\partial P))_{\mathbb{Z}}
, and so the divisor
\mathcal{D}_{\sigma}+\partial\mathcal{Y}_{\sigma}

is defined by
\vartheta_{p}=0
for
p\in C(\sigma)_{\mathbb{Z}}\setminus C(\partial\sigma)_{\mathbb{Z}}
.

By Lemma
7.11
, for every
p,q,r\in C(\sigma)_{\mathbb{Z}}
and
\beta\in H
, we have
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})=0
if
\beta\notin NE(\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma})
, and

N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X})=N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X}_{\sigma})
for every
\beta\in NE(\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\sigma})
. Hence,
\mathcal{Y}_{\sigma}
is the base change of
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}}
along
S_{H}\rightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}}
, compatibly with the theta functions. In particular, it follows from the above description of
\mathcal{D}_{\sigma}+\partial\mathcal{Y}_{\sigma}
in terms of theta functions that
\mathcal{D}_{\sigma}+\partial\mathcal{Y}_{\sigma}
is the base change of
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}}
, and, by Equation (
6.6
),
\mathcal{C}_{\sigma}:=\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{X}}|_{\mathcal{Y}_{\sigma}}

is the base change of
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}}

along
S_{H}\rightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}}
.

Finally, the map
S_{H}\rightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}}
in Equation (
7.1
) is a dominant toric morphism and so restricts to a morphism
T_{H}\rightarrow T_{\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}}
. By Theorem
7.7
ii) applied to the quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration
\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}\rightarrow\Delta
, the restriction
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}}|_{T_{\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}}}
is irreducible.
Hence, the restriction
\mathcal{Y}_{\sigma}|_{T_{H}}
is also irreducible, and we conclude that
\mathcal{Y}_{\sigma}
is an irreducible component of
\mathcal{Y}
.
∎

Theorem 7.13
.

Let
\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{X}}
be a quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration. Then,

(\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}},\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}}+\epsilon\,\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}})\rightarrow\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}}
is a family of KSBA stable pairs.

Proof.

By Theorem
7.6
, we already have that

(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{sec}},\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}})\longrightarrow S^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}}

is a projective flat family of semi-log-canonical surfaces
(Y_{t},D_{t})
such that
K_{Y_{t}}+D_{t}=0
.
In particular, the divisor
K_{Y_{t}}+D_{t}+\epsilon\,C_{t}=\epsilon\,C_{t}
is ample, and so it only remains to prove that the pairs
(Y_{t},D_{t}+\epsilon\,C_{t})
are semi-log-canonical.
It is enough to prove this over each of the torus orbits covering the toric stack
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}}
.

For a torus orbit corresponding to a cone of
\mathrm{MovSec}(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
, we can assume, up to replacing
\mathcal{X}
by another projective crepant resolution of
\overline{\mathcal{X}}
, that this cone is a face
H
of
NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
. By the description of
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}}|_{S_{H}}
given in
Theorem
7.12
i), and by
[
50
, Proposition 7.2]
, it is enough to show that for every 2-dimensional face
\sigma
of
NE(\mathcal{X}/\overline{\mathcal{X}})
, the restriction of
(\mathcal{Y}_{\sigma},\mathcal{D}_{\sigma}+\partial\mathcal{Y}_{\sigma}+\epsilon\,\mathcal{C}_{\sigma})
to
T_{H}
is a family of KSBA stable pairs.
As the map
S_{H}\rightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}}
in Equation (
7.1
) is a dominant toric morphism, it restricts to a morphism
T_{H}\rightarrow T_{\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}}
. Hence, by Theorem
7.12
ii), the restriction of
(\mathcal{Y}_{\sigma},\mathcal{D}_{\sigma}+\partial\mathcal{Y}_{\sigma}+\epsilon\,\mathcal{C}_{\sigma})
to
T_{H}
is a base change of the mirror family
(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}}+\epsilon\,\mathcal{C}_{X_{\sigma}})
restricted to
T_{\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}}
. By Theorem
7.7
ii) applied to the quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration
\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}\rightarrow\Delta
, the later family is a family of KSBA stable pairs and this concludes the proof in this case.

For a general torus orbit, we can assume as in Remark
6.29
that the corresponding cone is a bogus cone of the form
H+\sum_{v\in I}\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}[X^{v}]
, with
H
as above and
I
a subset
P_{\mathbb{Z}}
consisting of integral points
v
such that
X^{v}
is contracted by
f_{H}
. By Theorem
6.28
, the restriction of
(\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}},\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}}+\epsilon\,\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}})
to such a torus orbit is obtained
by
T_{0}
-equivariance from the restriction of
(\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}},\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}}+\epsilon\,\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}})
to
T_{H}
.
As the base change in Theorem
7.12
is compatible with the
T_{0}
-actions, it is enough to show that, for every 2-dimensional face
\sigma
of
\mathscr{P}_{H}
, the extension of the mirror family
(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}}+\epsilon\,\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}})
to the toric stratum corresponding to the bogus cone
\sum_{v\in I_{\sigma}}\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}[X^{v}_{\sigma}]
of
\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}
, where
I_{\sigma}=I\cap\sigma_{\mathbb{Z}}
, is a family of KSBA stable pairs. By Theorem
6.28
, any fiber
Y_{\sigma,t}
of this family is related by torus action, and so is isomorphic, to a fiber of

\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}}|_{T_{\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}}}
.
By Theorem
7.7
ii), such a fiber is normal, and so the KSBA stability follows by Theorem
7.7
i).
∎

8.
KSBA moduli spaces of stable log Calabi–Yau surfaces

Let
(Y,D,L)
be a polarized log Calabi–Yau surface and let
\mathscr{P}
be a good polyhedral decomposition of a Symington polytope
P
associated to
(Y,D,L)
as in §
2.3
. Let
\pi_{\mathscr{P}}\colon\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}\to\Delta
be the semistable mirror to the maximal degeneration of
(Y,D,L)
defined by
\mathscr{P}
, and denote by

(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}},\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}})\longrightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}}=\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[NE(\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}/\mathcal{\overline{X}}_{\mathscr{P}})]

the polarized mirror family as in Definition
6.17
. In what follows we refer to this family as the “double mirror” to
(Y,D,L)
. In this section we first investigate in Theorem
8.2
how the double mirror changes if
(P,\mathscr{P})
is rescaled. We then use this result to show in Theorem
8.6
that, up to rescaling
(P,\mathscr{P})
, the restriction of the double mirror to a particular one-dimensional locus is a toric degeneration.
Using this, we show in Theorem

8.10
that the general fiber of the double mirror is deformation equivalent to
(Y,D,L)
. Consequently, we prove in Theorem
8.11
that we obtain a surjective and finite map from the toric variety
S^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}}
to the coarse KSBA moduli space
M_{(Y,D,L)}
.

8.1.
Base change of open Kulikov degenerations

For every
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, let

\pi_{k\mathscr{P}}:\mathcal{X}_{k\mathscr{P}}\rightarrow\Delta
be the base change of the open Kulikov degeneration
\pi_{\mathscr{P}}\colon\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}\to\Delta
along the map

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\Delta

\displaystyle t

\displaystyle\longmapsto t^{k}\,.

Note that
\pi_{k\mathscr{P}}:\mathcal{X}_{k\mathscr{P}}\rightarrow\Delta
is not an open Kulikov degeneration in general, as the total space
\mathcal{X}_{k\mathscr{P}}
is singular.
Instead,
\pi_{k\mathscr{P}}:\mathcal{X}_{k\mathscr{P}}\rightarrow\Delta
is log smooth for the divisorial log structure on
\mathcal{X}_{k\mathscr{P}}
defined by the central fiber
\mathcal{X}_{k\mathscr{P},0}:=\pi_{k\mathscr{P}}^{-1}(0)
, and the divisorial log structure on
\Delta
defined by the origin
0\in\Delta
. Moreover, the dual intersection complex of the central fiber
\mathcal{X}_{k\mathscr{P},0}
is the scaled polyhedral decomposition
k\mathscr{P}
on the scaled polytope
kP
. The following result will allow us to construct an open Kulikov degeneration which is a resolution of singularities of
\pi_{k\mathscr{P}}:\mathcal{X}_{k\mathscr{P}}\rightarrow\Delta
.

Lemma 8.1
.

For every
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, there exists a good polyhedral decomposition
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}
on the scaled polytope
kP
, obtained as a refinement of the decomposition
k\mathscr{P}
on
kP
.

Proof.

We successively pull, as in the proof of Theorem
2.14
, all integral points in
kP
which are not vertices of
k\mathscr{P}
. This gives us a desired good polyhedral decomposition
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}
.
∎

Let
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}
be a good polyhedral decomposition as in Lemma
8.1
, and let
\pi_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}:\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}\rightarrow\Delta
be the corresponding open Kulikov degeneration.
As
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}
is a refinement of
k\mathscr{P}
, it induces a log modification,
that is a proper birational log étale log morphism as in
[
4
]
,
\nu:\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{k\mathscr{P}}
, such that
\pi_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}=\pi_{k\mathscr{P}}\circ\nu
. Let

\nu_{\star}:NE(\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}})\longrightarrow NE(\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathscr{P}})

be the composition of the pushforward of curve classes along
\nu
with the natural isomorphism

NE(\mathcal{X}_{k\mathscr{P}}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{k\mathscr{P}})\simeq NE(\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathscr{P}})
whose existence follows from the fact that the base change
t\mapsto t^{k}
does not change the central fiber, and so
\mathcal{X}_{k\mathscr{P},0}\simeq\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P},0}
. We denote by
\rho_{\nu}
the scheme morphism

\rho_{\nu}:\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[NE(\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathscr{P}})]\longrightarrow\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[NE(\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}})]

induced by
\nu_{\star}
.
The following result compares the polarized mirror family of
the open Kulikov degeneration
\pi_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}:\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}\rightarrow\Delta

with the polarized mirror family of the open Kulikov degeneration
\pi_{\mathscr{P}}:\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}\rightarrow\Delta

Theorem 8.2
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
and
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}
be a good polyhedral decomposition of the scaled polytope
kP
, obtained as a refinement of
k\mathscr{P}
.
Then,
the mirror family

(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[NE(\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}/\mathcal{\overline{X}}_{\mathscr{P}})]

of the quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration
\pi_{\mathscr{P}}:\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}\rightarrow\Delta

is the base change
along the map

\rho_{\nu}:\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[NE(\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathscr{P}})]\longrightarrow\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[NE(\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}})]

of the mirror family

(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[NE(\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}/\mathcal{\overline{X}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}})]

of
the quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration
\pi_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}:\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}\rightarrow\Delta
.
Moreover, the pullback of the line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}}

by the induced map

\tilde{\rho}_{\nu}:\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}}

is isomorphic to
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}}^{\otimes k}
, that is, we have
\tilde{\rho}_{\nu}^{\star}\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}}\simeq\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}}^{\otimes k}
.

Proof.

It suffices to identify the theta bases and the structure constants of the mirror algebra of
\pi_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}:\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}\rightarrow\Delta
with the generators and structure constants of the subalgebra of the mirror algebra of
\pi_{\mathscr{P}}:\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}\rightarrow\Delta
consisting of elements of degree divisible by
k
.
To identify the theta bases, one notes that the index set of the theta basis for the mirror algebra of
\pi_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}:\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}\rightarrow\Delta
is
C(kP)_{\mathbb{Z}}
, which is exactly the set of integral points of height divisible by
k
in the cone
C(P)_{\mathbb{Z}}
indexing the theta basis for the mirror algebra of
\pi_{\mathscr{P}}:\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}\rightarrow\Delta
.
Hence, it remains to show that, for every
p,q,r\in C(kP)_{\mathbb{Z}}
and
\beta\in NE(\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathscr{P}})
, we have

(8.1)

N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta^{\prime}\in NE(\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}})\\
\nu_{\star}\beta^{\prime}=\beta\end{subarray}}N_{pqr}^{\beta^{\prime}}(\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}})\,.

To prove Equation (
8.1
), we first note that, even if the base change
\mathcal{X}_{k\mathscr{P}}
of
\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}
is not the total space of an open Kulikov degeneration, it is still log smooth over
\Delta
, and so one can also define invariants
N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X}_{k\mathscr{P}})
for
\mathcal{X}_{k\mathscr{P}}
. As the base change
t\mapsto t^{k}
does not change the central fiber and only scales the log structure, it follows that we have

(8.2)

N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}})=N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X}_{k\mathscr{P}})

for all
p,q,r\in C(kP)_{\mathbb{Z}}
and
\beta\in NE(\mathcal{X}_{k\mathscr{P}}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{k\mathscr{P}})\simeq NE(\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathscr{P}})
.
On the other hand, as
\nu:\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{k\mathscr{P}}
is a log modification, it follows from
[
56
, §10]
that

(8.3)

N_{pqr}^{\beta}(\mathcal{X}_{k\mathscr{P}})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta^{\prime}\in NE(\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}})\\
\nu_{\star}\beta^{\prime}=\beta\end{subarray}}N_{pqr}^{\beta^{\prime}}(\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}})\,,

for all
p,q,r\in C(kP)_{\mathbb{Z}}
and
\beta\in NE(\mathcal{X}_{k\mathscr{P}}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{k\mathscr{P}})
. Since Equation (
8.1
) follows from Equations (
8.2
) and (
8.3
), this concludes the proof.
∎

8.2.
From semistable mirrors to toric degenerations

Let
(Y,D,L)
be a polarized log Calabi–Yau surface with a maximal degeneration defined by
(P,\mathscr{P})
as in §
2.4
and let
\pi_{\mathscr{P}}:\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}\to\Delta
be the corresponding semistable mirror as in §
4.3
.

We use the notation introduced in the construction of
(P,\mathscr{P})
in §
2.2
.
For every
1\leq i\leq n
and
1\leq j\leq N_{i}
, consider the image
L_{ij}
in
P
of the line in
\overline{P}
passing through
p_{ij}
, and parallel to the edge
\overline{P}_{i}
of
\overline{P}
.
By Lemma
2.11
, the intersection of
L_{ij}
with the interior of
P
is an interval of positive length, and so one can find a rational point
p_{ij}^{\prime}\in L_{ij}

close enough to
p_{ij}
in the sense that the smallest cell of
\mathscr{P}
containing
p_{ij}^{\prime}
also contains
p_{ij}
, and such that all the points
p_{ij}^{\prime}
are distinct and contained in the interior of
P
.

Fix a large enough integer
k
such that all the points
p_{ij}^{\prime}
become integral points of the scaled polytope
kP
. Arguing as in the proof of Lemma
8.1
and pulling first the points
p_{ij}^{\prime}
among the integral points of
kP
which are not vertices of
k\mathscr{P}
, we obtain a
good polyhedral decomposition
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}
on
kP
, obtained as a refinement of
k\mathscr{P}
, and such that every line segment
[p_{ij},p_{ij}^{\prime}]
is a union of edges of
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}
.

Figure 8.1.
On the left, triangles
\Delta_{ij}
cut out from
\overline{P}
. In the center, induced cuts in
P
. On the right, deformed cuts
\delta_{ij}
.

Continuously moving the tips of the cut triangles
\Delta_{ij}
on the line
L_{ij}
away from
p_{ij}

towards
p_{ij}^{\prime}

produces a deformation of the integral affine structure on
P
, with endpoint an integral affine structure with distinct focus-focus singularities
\widetilde{p}_{ij}
– see Figure
8.1
. Moreover, we can assume that
\widetilde{p}_{ij}
is contained in the interior of the edge of
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}
containing
p_{ij}
and contained in
[p_{ij},p_{ij}^{\prime}]
.
In particular, the cuts induced by the triangles
\Delta_{ij}
deform into lines
\delta_{ij}
connecting the side
\overline{P}_{i}
to the focus-focus singularities
\widetilde{p}_{ij}
.
Every integral point
v\in P_{\mathbb{Z}}
is smooth for the deformed integral affine structure, and so its star defines a toric fan
\Sigma_{v}
. Note that, for every
i,j
such that
v=p_{ij}
, the line segment

[p_{ij},p_{ij}^{\prime}]
corresponds to a ray

\rho_{ij}
of the fan
\Sigma_{v}
.

Lemma 8.3
.

For every integral point
v\in(kP)_{\mathbb{Z}}
, the log Calabi–Yau surface
(X^{v}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}},\partial X^{v}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}})
is deformation-equivalent to the surface obtained from the toric surface
(X_{\Sigma_{v}},\partial X_{\Sigma_{v}})
with fan
\Sigma_{v}
by blowing up, for every ray
\rho
of
\Sigma_{v}
,
n_{\rho}
distinct smooth points on the corresponding toric divisor
D_{\rho}
, where
n_{\rho}
is the number of pairs
(i,j)
with
p_{ij}=v
and
\rho_{ij}=\rho
.

Proof.

Recall from §
4.2
that the log Calabi–Yau surface
(X^{v}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}},\partial X^{v}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}})
is a smoothing of the toric surface
(X^{v}_{n\mathscr{P}},\partial X^{v}_{n\mathscr{P}})
with fan the star of
v
in
\overline{P}
.
Using the almost toric description of smoothings of quotient singularities
– see
[
91
, §6.1]
and
[
32
, Remark 2.7]
, it follows that
(X^{v}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}},\partial X^{v}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}})
admits an almost toric fibration over a Symington polytope obtained from a toric polytope of
(X^{v}_{k\overline{\mathscr{P}}},\partial X^{v}_{k\overline{\mathscr{P}}})

by node smoothing. Therefore, by the combinatorial duality between internal blow-ups and node smoothings – see e.g.
[
30
, p 488]
, the pair

(X^{v}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}},\partial X^{v}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}})
is diffeomorphic, and so deformation-equivalent by
[
34
, Theorem 5.14]
, to the log Calabi–Yau surface described in Lemma
8.3
as internal blow-ups of the toric surface
(X_{\Sigma_{v}},\partial X_{\Sigma_{v}})
.
∎

We denote
by
\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}},0}^{gs}

the normal crossing surface obtained from
\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}},0}
by blowing down all the
(-1)
-curves as in Lemma
8.3
.
We show below that
\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}},0}^{gs}
is a central fiber of a toric degeneration
\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}^{gs}\rightarrow\Delta
in the sense of

[
47
]
, that is, with toric irreducible components of the central fiber glued along toric divisors, and with the map to
\Delta
étale locally given by a toric morphism near the 0-dimensional strata of the central fiber.

Lemma 8.4
.

The blowdown morphism

p_{0}:\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}},0}\to\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}},0}^{gs}
is projective and deforms to a projective birational morphism
p:\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}\to\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}^{gs}
, contracting finitely many internal exceptional curves of the central fiber, and fitting into a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}^{gs}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}^{gs}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where
\pi_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}^{gs}:\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}^{gs}\to\Delta
is a toric degeneration in the sense of

[
47
]
.

Proof.

Since
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}
is a good polyhedral decomposition on
kP
as in §
2.3
, there is a strictly convex integral multi-valued PL function
\varphi
on
kP
whose domains of linearity agree with
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}
, as in §
2.4
.
Then, we obtain from
\varphi
a strictly convex integral multi-valued PL function
\varphi^{gs}
on
kP
on the integral affine manifold with singularities
(kP,\widetilde{\mathscr{P}})^{gs}
obtained from
(kP,\widetilde{\mathscr{P}})
by pushing singularities away from the vertices as described before Lemma
8.3
– see
[
18
, §3]
where such PL functions on integral affine manifolds with singularities pushed away are studied in detail. Therefore, by
[
47
, Theorem 2.34]
since
P
is a disk with second cohomology zero,
\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}},0}^{gs}
is a quasi-projective scheme. Hence, we have an ample line bundle
L_{0}
on
\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}},0}^{gs}
whose pullback under
\pi_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}
is a nef line bundle on
\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}
.
Since
H^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}},0},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}},0}})=0

by Lemma
3.10
, this line bundle deforms to a nef line bundle
L
on
\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}
. Since
\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}
is a Mori dream space by Proposition
3.36
, there is a projective birational morphism

p:\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}^{gs}
exactly contracting the curves
C
with
L\cdot C=0
, that is the finitely many internal exceptional curves in the central fiber contracted by
p_{0}
.

By construction, the irreducible components of the central fiber
\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}},0}^{gs}
are the toric varieties
X_{\Sigma_{v}}
as in Lemma
8.3
and are glued along toric divisors. Moreover,
\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}^{gs}
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}
away from the finitely many ordinary double points where the internal exceptional curves are contracted, and so in particular in the neighborhood of 0-dimensional strata of the central fiber. As
\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}\rightarrow\Delta
is log smooth, it follows that
\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}^{gs}\rightarrow\Delta
is also log smooth near the 0-dimensional strata of the central fiber, and we conclude that
\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}^{gs}\rightarrow\Delta
is a toric degeneration in the sense of
[
47
]
.
∎

8.3.
Toric degenerations and intrinsic mirrors

By Lemma
8.4
, for
k
large enough, there exists a refinement
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}
of the scaled polyhedral decomposition
k\mathscr{P}
such that the corresponding quasi-projective Kulikov degeneration
\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}\rightarrow\Delta
admits a birational contraction to a toric degeneration
\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}^{gs}\rightarrow\Delta
in the sense of
[
47
]
. A mirror construction for so-called positive and simple polarized toric degenerations of Calabi–Yau varieties has been introduced by Gross–Siebert
[
47
]
– see
[
39
]
for an exposition in dimension two. The toric degeneration
\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}^{gs}\rightarrow\Delta

is an example of positive and simple toric degeneration, since its dual intersection complex is an integral affine manifold with only focus-focus singularities localized in the interior of the edges of the polyhedral decomposition.
Unlike the intrinsic mirror construction for log smooth degenerations
[
48
,
49
]
reviewed in §
5
and used in §
6
-
7
, which is based on the enumerative geometry of punctured Gromov–Witten invariants, the mirror construction for toric degenerations is based on a scattering diagram recursively constructed from explicit initial data, and so is mostly combinatorial. In particular, it is often easier to determine the output of the mirror construction for toric degenerations. In this section, we compare the intrinsic mirror family
(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}},\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}})
to
\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}\rightarrow\Delta
with the mirror to the toric degeneration
\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}^{gs}\rightarrow\Delta
. Similar comparison results have been obtained in
[
41
, §3.3]
,
[
16
]
,
[
71
, §6.1]
,
[
17
, §2]
.

Let
G
be the face of
NE(\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}})
spanned by the finitely many internal exceptional curves blow down by

p:\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}^{gs}

as in Lemmas
8.3
and
8.4
.
Consider the monoid ideal
J_{G}=NE(\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}})\setminus G
, and similarly as in
[
41
, Definition 3.14]
define the
Gross–Siebert locus
as the open torus
T^{gs}=\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[G^{\mathrm{gp}}]
in the toric stratum

\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[G]=\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[NE(\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}})/J_{G}]
of the toric variety
S_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}}
.

Lemma 8.5
.

The mirror family
(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}}+\epsilon\,\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}})
is constant with fiber
Y_{0_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}}}
in restriction to
\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[G]
, and so to
T^{gs}
.

Proof.

As in the proof of Lemma
7.2
, all the wall-crossing transformations in the canonical scattering diagrams are trivial modulo
J_{G}
. Moreover, as no curve of the double locus of
\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}},0}
is contracted by
p
, all the kinks in the canonical scattering diagram are also trivial modulo
J_{G}
.
∎

The map
p:\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}^{gs}
induces a natural inclusion
NE(\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}})\subset NE(\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}^{gs}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}})\oplus G^{\mathrm{gp}}
, and so a morphism

\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[NE(\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}^{gs}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}})]\times T^{gs}\longrightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}}\,.

Fixing an ample divisor
H
on
\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}^{gs}
defines an affine line

(\mathbb{A}^{1})_{H}\subset\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[NE(\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}^{gs}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}})]\,.

Also fixing a point
a
in
T^{gs}
, and denoting
(\mathbb{A}^{1})_{H,a}:=(\mathbb{A}^{1})_{H}\times\{a\}
, we obtain by restriction a map

(8.4)

(\mathbb{A}^{1})_{H,a}\longrightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}}\,.

On the other hand, the choice of
H
naturally defines a strictly convex multivalued PL function
\varphi^{gs}_{H}
on the integral affine manifold with singularities
(kP,\widetilde{\mathscr{P}})^{gs}
obtained from
(kP,\widetilde{\mathscr{P}})
by generically pushing away the singularities from the vertices to irrational points on edges of
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}
as described before Lemma
8.3
. Indeed, by standard toric geometry, the ample divisor
H
restricted to each of the toric irreducible components of
\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}},0}^{gs}
defines strictly convex PL functions around the various vertices of
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}
. Moreover, the choice of the point
a\in T^{gs}
, viewed as an algebra homomorphism
a:\mathbb{C}[G^{gp}]\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
, naturally defines functions
1+a(t^{E_{i}})z^{\pm m_{i}}
attached to the monodromy invariant directions
\pm m_{i}
of the focus-focus singularity associated to the contracted curve
E_{i}
.
Taking as input the integral affine
(kP,\widetilde{\mathscr{P}})^{gs}
, having only focus-focus singularities placed at irrational points on the edges of
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}
, the strictly convex multivalued PL function
\varphi^{gs}_{H}
, and the functions
1+a(t^{E_{i}})z^{\pm m_{i}}
as “slab functions”, the mirror construction for toric degeneration of
[
47
]
– see also
[
39
, Chapter 6]
, produces a projective family
\hat{\mathcal{Y}}^{gs}_{H,a}\rightarrow\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[\![t]\!]
, with a divisor
\hat{\mathcal{D}}_{H,a}^{gs}
, and an ample line bundle
\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{gs}_{H,a}
.

Theorem 8.6
.

For every ample divisor
H
on
\mathcal{X}^{gs}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}
, and every point
a\in T^{gs}
, the mirror family
(\hat{\mathcal{Y}}^{gs}_{H,a},\hat{\mathcal{D}}_{H,a}^{gs},\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{gs}_{H,a})\rightarrow\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[\![t]\!]
of the toric degeneration
\mathcal{X}^{gs}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}\rightarrow\Delta
canonically extends to a family

(\mathcal{Y}^{gs}_{H,a},\mathcal{D}_{H,a}^{gs},\mathcal{L}^{gs}_{H,a})\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}
, which is isomorphic to the pullback, along the map

(\mathbb{A}^{1})_{H,a}\longrightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}}

in (
8.4
), of the mirror family
(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}},\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}})\rightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}}
of the quasi-projective open Kulikov degeneration
\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}\rightarrow\Delta
.

Proof.

The mirror family of the toric degeneration
\mathcal{X}^{gs}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}\rightarrow\Delta
can be computed in terms of theta functions defined using broken lines in a scattering diagram
\mathfrak{D}^{gs}
on
(kP,\widetilde{\mathscr{P}})^{gs}
, constructed by the Kontsevich-Soibelman algorithm from explicit initial walls coming out of the focus-focus singularities – see
[
39
, §6.3]
for the construction of the scattering diagram and
[
45
, Appendix A]
for a discussion of theta functions. On the other hand, as reviewed in §
5.3
, the mirror family of

\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}\rightarrow\Delta
can be computed in terms of theta functions defined using broken lines in the canonical scattering diagram

\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}}
.
Arguing as in
[
41
, §3]
, replacing the use of

[
46
]
, which applies to a single log Calabi–Yau surface, by
[
40
, §8]
, which applies to a normal crossing union of log Calabi–Yau surfaces such as
\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}},0}
, one shows that
\mathfrak{D}^{gs}
can be naturally realized as a deformation of
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}}
obtained by pushing the focus-focus singularities away from the vertices of the triangulation
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}
. Moreover, as in
[
41
, Lemma 3.28]
, there is a natural one-to-one correspondence between broken lines and so a natural identification of the corresponding theta functions. The convergence of the algebra of theta functions for
\hat{\mathcal{Y}}^{gs}_{H,a}\rightarrow\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}[\![t]\!]
then follows from the convergence of the algebra of theta functions for
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}}\rightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}}
.
∎

8.4.
The general fiber of the mirror family

Let
(Y,D,L)
be a polarized log Calabi–Yau surface and let
(P,\mathscr{P})
be an associated Symington polytope with a good polyhedral decomposition as in Construction
2.10
. Let
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}
be a refinement of
k\mathscr{P}
for large
k
as in Lemma
8.4
.
Let
\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}^{gs}\rightarrow\Delta
be the corresponding toric degeneration given by Lemma
8.4
, and

(\mathcal{Y}^{gs}_{H,a},\mathcal{D}_{H,a}^{gs},\mathcal{L}^{gs}_{H,a})\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}
the corresponding mirror family given by Theorem
8.6
, with intersection complex the integral affine manifold with singularities
(kP)^{gs}
obtained from
kP
by pushing the focus-focus singularities away from the vertices as in
§
8.2
.

The integral affine manifold with singularities
(kP)^{gs}
is naturally an almost toric base as in
[
91
]
. Let
(Y^{sym},D^{sym},L^{sym})\rightarrow(kP)^{gs}
be the corresponding almost toric fibration given by
[
91
, Corollary 4.2]
. This fibration is constructed locally as a toric momentum map fibration away from the focus-focus singularities, and using an explicit local model described in
[
91
, §4.2]
around the focus-focus singularities. In particular,
Y^{sym}
is naturally a differentiable orbifold,
with orbifold points in one-to-one correspondence with the vertices of
P
, and locally given by the toric surface singularities with momentum polytopes given by the cones locally describing
P
near its vertices.

Lemma 8.7
.

The surface
(Y,D,L^{\otimes k})
admits an almost toric fibration over
(kP)^{gs}

and is diffeomorphic as an orbifold to
(Y^{sym},D^{sym},L^{sym})
.

Proof.

By the Construction
2.10
of
(P,\mathscr{P})
from a toric model of
(Y,D,L)
,
(kP)^{gs}
is a Symington polytope for
(Y,D,L^{\otimes k})
, and so, it follows from
[
91
, Corollary 4.2]
that
(Y,D,L^{\otimes k})

admits an almost toric fibration over

(kP)^{gs}

and so is diffeomorphic as an orbifold to
(Y^{sym},D^{sym},L^{sym})
.
∎

Lemma 8.8
.

The general fiber
(Y^{gs}_{t},D^{gs}_{t},L^{gs}_{t})
of

(\mathcal{Y}^{gs}_{H,a},\mathcal{D}_{H,a}^{gs},\mathcal{L}^{gs}_{H,a})\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}

is diffeomorphic as an orbifold to
(Y^{sym},D^{sym},L^{sym})
.

Proof.

The toric mirror degeneration
(\mathcal{Y}^{gs}_{H,a},\mathcal{D}_{H,a}^{gs},\mathcal{L}^{gs}_{H,a})\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}
admits a gluing description in terms of local charts – see
[
39
, §6.2.5]
for details. We claim that it follows from this description that a general fiber
(Y^{gs}_{t},D^{gs}_{t},L^{gs}_{t})
admits an almost toric fibration over
(kP)^{gs}
, and so is diffeomorphic as an orbifold to
(Y^{sym},D^{sym},L^{sym})

by
[
91
, Corollary 4.2]
. Indeed, away from the finitely many ordinary double points corresponding to focus-focus singularities of
(kP)^{gs}
, the family
(\mathcal{Y}^{gs}_{H,a},\mathcal{D}_{H,a}^{gs},\mathcal{L}^{gs}_{H,a})\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}
is toric, and the torus fibration over the smooth part of
(kP)^{gs}
is obtained by locally toric deformation of the torus fibrations on the toric irreducible components of the central fiber induced by the moment maps. Moreover, each of the ordinary double points admits a neighborhood locally described by the equation

(8.5)

xy=t(1+w)

where the projection to
\mathbb{A}^{1}
is given by the
t
-coordinate. There is an explicit torus fibration with a single nodal fiber on the corresponding general fiber of (
8.5
), which is studied in detail in
[
22
, §5.1]
– see also
[
24
, Example 3.20]
[
14
, Example 1.8]
. Analogously as in the proof of
[
24
, Theorem 3.22]
, this fibration can be glued in a smooth way to the smooth torus fibration over the smooth locus of
(kP)^{gs}
.
∎

Theorem 8.9
.

The general fiber
(Y^{gs}_{t},D^{gs}_{t},L^{gs}_{t})
of

(\mathcal{Y}^{gs}_{H,a},\mathcal{D}_{H,a}^{gs},\mathcal{L}^{gs}_{H,a})\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}

is deformation equivalent to
(Y,D,L^{\otimes k})
.

Proof.

Combining Lemma
8.7
and Lemma
8.8
, we obtain that

(Y^{gs}_{t},D^{gs}_{t},L^{gs}_{t})
and
(Y,D,L^{\otimes k})
are diffeomorphic as orbifolds. Moreover, the orbifold points of both

(Y^{gs}_{t},D^{gs}_{t},L^{gs}_{t})
and
(Y,D,L^{\otimes k})

have Zariski neighborhoods
isomorphic to the toric surface singularities with momentum polytopes given by the cones locally describing
P

near its vertices. Indeed, this follows
for
(Y,D,L^{\otimes k})

from the Construction
2.10
of
(P,\mathscr{P})
from a toric model of
(Y,D,L)
, and for

(Y^{gs}_{t},D^{gs}_{t},L^{gs}_{t})
from the explicit gluing construction of the mirror to a toric degenerations by gluing local models – see
[
39
, §6.2.5]
.

Hence, one can torically resolve in an identical way the orbifold points of both
(Y^{gs}_{t},D^{gs}_{t},L^{gs}_{t})
and
(Y,D,L^{\otimes k})
to obtain diffeomorphic smooth log Calabi–Yau surfaces
(\widetilde{Y}^{gs}_{t},\widetilde{D}^{gs}_{t},\widetilde{L}^{gs}_{t})
and
(\widetilde{Y},\widetilde{D},\widetilde{L}^{\otimes k})
.
Therefore, by
[
34
, Theorem 5.14]
, the smooth log Calabi–Yau surfaces
(\widetilde{Y}^{gs}_{t},\widetilde{D}^{gs}_{t})
and
(\widetilde{Y},\widetilde{D})
are deformation equivalent. Moreover, for a smooth log Calabi–Yau surface, one has
H^{1}(\mathcal{O})=0
, and so algebraic line bundles are classified up to algebraic isomorphism by their first Chern classes. It follows that the line bundles
\widetilde{L}^{gs}_{t}
and
\widetilde{L}^{\otimes k}
are isomorphic under the deformation equivalence between

(\widetilde{Y}^{gs}_{t},\widetilde{D}^{gs}_{t})
and
(\widetilde{Y},\widetilde{D})
. Finally,
Theorem
8.9
follows by contracting back the exceptional divisors obtained while resolving the orbifold points.
∎

Theorem 8.10
.

Let
(Y,D,L)
be a polarized log Calabi–Yau surface and let
(P,\mathscr{P})
be an associated Symington polytope with a good polyhedral decomposition as in Construction
2.10
. Then, for general
t
in the open torus
T_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}}\subset S_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}}
, the fiber
(Y_{t},D_{t},L_{t})
over
t
of the double mirror
(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}},\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}})\rightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}}
is deformation equivalent to
(Y,D,L)
.

Proof.

By Theorem
8.6
, there exists a fiber of the double mirror
(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}},\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}})\rightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}}
which is a general fiber of one of the mirror families
(\mathcal{Y}^{gs}_{H,a},\mathcal{D}_{H,a}^{gs},\mathcal{L}^{gs}_{H,a})\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}
. Hence, it follows from Theorem
8.9
that the general fiber of the double mirror
(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}},\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}})\rightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}}
is deformation equivalent to
(Y,D,L^{\otimes k})
.
On the other hand, denoting by
(Y_{t},D_{t},L_{t})
a general fiber of the double mirror
(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}},\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}})\rightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}}
, it follows from Theorem
8.2
that

(Y_{t},D_{t},L_{t}^{\otimes k})
is a fiber of the double mirror of
(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}},\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}})\rightarrow S_{\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}}
, and so is also deformation equivalent to
(Y,D,L^{\otimes k})
. Finally, as
\mathrm{Pic}(Y)
is torsion-free by
Lemma
2.9
,
L_{t}^{\otimes k}\simeq L^{\otimes k}
implies
L_{t}\simeq L
, and this concludes the proof of Theorem
8.10
.
∎

8.5.
The Hacking–Keel–Yu conjecture

Putting together our results in previous sections, we finally arrive at the following theorem, proving the Hacking–Keel–Yu conjecture.

Theorem 8.11
.

Let
(Y,D,L)
be a polarized log Calabi–Yau surface with a maximal degeneration defined by
(P,\mathscr{P})
as in §
2.4
, and let
{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}}
be its semistable mirror. Then, the coarse KSBA moduli space
M_{(Y,D,L)}
admits a toric finite cover

S_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}}^{\mathrm{sec}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{(Y,D,L)}
,
where
S_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}}^{\mathrm{sec}}
is the toric variety associated to the secondary fan
\mathrm{Sec}(\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathscr{P}})
as in Definition
6.26
.

Proof.

By Theorem
7.13
,
the toric stack

\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}}
is the base of a family of KSBA stable log Calabi–Yau surfaces which by Theorem
8.10
are generically deformation equivalent to
(Y,D,L)
. Hence, by the universal property of the KSBA moduli space
\mathcal{M}_{(Y,D,L)}
we obtain a map of stacks
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}}\to\mathcal{M}_{(Y,D,L)}
, and so an induced map
S^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}}\to M_{(Y,D,L)}
at the level of coarse moduli spaces.

To prove that this map is finite, it is enough by
[
50
, Lemma 8.7]
to show that no 1-dimensional toric stratum of
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}}
is contracted to a point, that is, that
(\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{D}+\epsilon\,\mathcal{C})
is a non-constant family of KSBA stable pairs in restriction to every 1-dimensional toric stratum of
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}}
.
Let
\rho_{0}
be a codimension one cone of the secondary fan
\mathrm{Sec}(\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathscr{P}})
, corresponding to a 1-dimensional toric stratum
S_{\rho_{0}}
of
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}}
. As the fan
\mathrm{Sec}(\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathscr{P}})
is complete, there are two maximal cones
\rho_{1}
and
\rho_{2}
containing
\rho_{0}
, corresponding to the two 0-dimensional toric strata
S_{\rho_{1}}
and
S_{\rho_{2}}
contained in
S_{\rho_{0}}
. We will show that at least one of the fibers
(Y_{\rho_{1}},D_{\rho_{1}})
or
(Y_{\rho_{2}},D_{\rho_{2}})
of
(\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{D})
over the points
S_{\rho_{1}}
and
S_{\rho_{2}}
is not isomorphic to the general fiber
(Y_{\rho_{0}},D_{\rho_{0}})
of
(\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{D})
over
S_{\rho_{0}}
.

Let
\tilde{\rho}_{0}
be a codimension one cone of the Mori fan containing
\rho_{0}
, and
\tilde{\rho}_{1}
,
\tilde{\rho}_{2}
be the two maximal dimensional cones of the Mori fan containing
\tilde{\rho}_{0}
and contained in

\rho_{1}
and
\rho_{2}
respectively.
For
i=0,1,2
, we have
\tilde{\rho}_{i}=\mathrm{Nef}(\mathcal{Z}_{i}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathscr{P}})+\sum_{v\in J_{i}}\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}[X^{v}]
, where
\mathcal{Z}_{i}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathscr{P}}
is a partial crepant resolution, and
X^{v}
with
v\in J_{i}
are divisors which are contracted by a crepant resolution of
\mathcal{Z}_{i}
.
Moreover, since
\tilde{\rho}_{0}
is a codimension one face of
\tilde{\rho}_{i}
, we have a birational contraction
\mathcal{Z}_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}_{0}
, and either this map is of relative Picard rank one and
J_{i}=J_{0}
, or this map is an isomorphism and
J_{i}=J_{0}\sqcup\{V\}
for some
V\in J_{i}\setminus J_{0}
.
The later case cannot happen for both
i=1
and
i=2
:
indeed, if
J_{1}=J_{0}\sqcup\{V\}
, then
[X^{V}]\in\tilde{\rho}_{1}
and
[X^{V}]\notin\tilde{\rho}_{0}
, and so
[X^{V}]\notin\tilde{\rho}_{2}
.

Hence, without loss of generality, we can assume that
\mathcal{Z}_{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}_{0}
is of relative Picard rank one and
J_{1}=J_{0}
. Let
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}_{1}
be a projective crepant resolution. Denote by
H_{1}
the face of
NE(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathscr{P}})
corresponding to
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}_{1}
, and by
H_{0}
the codimension one face of
H_{1}
corresponding to
\mathcal{Z}_{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}_{0}
.
By Theorem
7.12
, the intersection complex of
(Y_{\rho_{1}},D_{\rho_{1}})

(resp.
(Y_{\rho_{0}},D_{\rho_{0}})
) is given by the polyhedral decomposition
\mathscr{P}_{H_{1}}
(resp.
\mathscr{P}_{H_{0}}
) associated to
H_{1}
(resp .
H_{0}
) as in
§
7.3
.

Assume first that
\mathcal{Z}_{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}_{0}
is an isomorphism in codimension one. If
\mathcal{Z}_{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}_{0}
contracts a curve in the double locus of
\mathcal{Z}_{1}
, then the corresponding edge in
\mathscr{P}_{H_{1}}
disappears in
\mathscr{P}_{H_{0}}
. Hence,
Y_{\rho_{1}}
, and
Y_{\rho_{0}}
are not isomorphic, and the result is proved in this case. On the other hand, if
\mathcal{Z}_{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}_{0}
does not contract any curve in the double locus of
\mathcal{Z}_{1}
, then the exceptional locus of
\mathcal{Z}_{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}_{0}
is a rigid internal curve in an irreducible component of
\mathcal{Z}_{1}
, whose strict transform in
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
is necessarily an internal exceptional curve. It follows that
NE(\mathcal{Z}_{0}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathscr{P}})
is contained in the codimension one face of
NE(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathscr{P}})

defined by the M1 flop defined by contracting this internal exceptional curve, which is by Lemma
6.19
in contradiction with the assumption that
\rho_{0}
is a codimension cone of the secondary fan, and so this last case actually does not occur.

Thus, it remains to treat the case where
\mathcal{Z}_{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}_{0}
is a divisorial contraction.
Since
\mathcal{Z}_{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}_{0}
is of relative Picard rank one, its exceptional locus consists of a single divisor
Z_{1}^{v}
corresponding to a vertex
v
of
\mathscr{P}_{H_{1}}
. If an irreducible component of the boundary of
Z_{1}^{v}
is contracted into
\mathcal{Z}_{0}
,
then the corresponding edge of
\mathscr{P}_{H_{1}}
disappears in
\mathscr{P}_{H_{0}}
, and so we also deduce in this case that
Y_{\rho_{1}}
and
Y_{\rho_{0}}
are not isomorphic.
If no boundary component of
Z_{1}^{v}
is contracted in
\mathcal{Z}_{0}
, then, as in
[
50
, §8.2]
,
Z_{1}^{v}
is necessarily a generically
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle over
\mathbb{P}^{1}
, contracted to its base
\mathbb{P}^{1}
in
\mathcal{Z}_{0}
, and its boundary
\partial Z_{v}^{1}
is either an irreducible nodal curve mapping 2:1 to the base, or the union of two sections intersecting in two points. If
\partial Z_{v}^{1}
is the union of two irreducible components
D_{1}
and
D_{2}
, consider the corresponding edges
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
adjacent to
v
in
\mathscr{P}_{H_{1}}
, and choose a 2-dimensional face
\sigma_{1}
of
\mathscr{P}_{H_{1}}
such that
E_{1}\cup E_{2}
are edges of the boundary of
\sigma_{1}
. Denote by
\sigma_{0}
the same face viewed as a 2-dimensional face of
\mathscr{P}_{H_{0}}
.
By Theorem
7.12
, there is an irreducible component
(Y_{\sigma_{1}},\partial Y_{\sigma_{1}})
(resp.
(Y_{\sigma_{0}},\partial Y_{\sigma_{0}})
) of
Y_{\rho_{1}}
(resp.
Y_{\rho_{0}}
)
isomorphic to the general fiber of the mirror family to the open Kulikov degeneration
\mathcal{X}_{\sigma_{1}}
(resp.
\mathcal{X}_{\sigma_{0}}
) with tropicalization
