(C(\sigma_{1}),C(\mathscr{P}_{\sigma_{1}}))
(resp.
(C(\sigma_{1}),C(\mathscr{P}_{\sigma_{1}}))
).
The vertex
v
can be viewed as a boundary vertex of both
\mathscr{P}_{\sigma_{1}}
and
\mathscr{P}_{\sigma_{0}}
. Since the
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-fibers of
Z^{1}_{v}
are not contracted in
\mathcal{Z}_{1}
, the non-compact irreducible component corresponding to
v
in the central fiber
\mathcal{X}_{\sigma_{1},0}
of
\mathcal{X}_{\sigma_{1}}
is a
0
-surface. Hence, by Lemma
6.24
, it corresponds to a singular point of
\partial Y_{\sigma_{1}}
. On the other hand, since the
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-fibers of
Z^{1}_{v}
are contracted in
\mathcal{Z}_{0}
, the non-compact irreducible component corresponding to
v
in the central fiber
\mathcal{X}_{\sigma_{0},0}
of
\mathcal{X}_{\sigma_{0}}
is a
1
-surface.
Thus, by Lemma
6.24
, it corresponds to a smooth point of
\partial Y_{\sigma_{0}}
. Since a singular point of
\partial Y_{\sigma_{1}}
becomes a smooth point in
\partial Y_{\sigma_{0}}
, it follows that
Y_{\rho_{1}}
and
Y_{\rho_{0}}
are not isomorphic.

Finally, the case where
\partial Z^{1}_{v}
is irreducible follows from the previous case as in
[
50
, §8.2]
. Indeed, denoting by
\sigma_{1}
(resp.
\sigma_{0}
) the 2-dimensional face of
\mathscr{P}_{H_{1}}
(resp.
\mathscr{P}_{H_{0}}
) containing
v
, and cutting
\sigma_{1}
(resp.
\sigma_{0}
) along the edges of
\mathscr{P}_{\sigma_{1}}
(resp.
\mathscr{P}_{\sigma_{0}}
) connecting
v
to the boundary of
\sigma_{1}
(resp.
\sigma_{0}
), we obtain an integral affine manifold with singularities
(\tilde{\sigma}_{1},\mathscr{P}_{\tilde{\sigma}_{1}})

(resp.
(\tilde{\sigma}_{0},\mathscr{P}_{\tilde{\sigma}_{0}})
)
with the same formal properties as
(\sigma_{1},\mathscr{P}_{\sigma_{1}})

(resp.
(\sigma_{0},\mathscr{P}_{\sigma_{0}})
)
in the case where
\partial Z^{1}_{v}
is reducible. There is a natural open Kulikov degeneration
\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\sigma}_{1}}
(resp.
\mathcal{X}_{\widetilde{\sigma}_{0}}
) with tropicalization
(C(\widetilde{\sigma}_{1}),C(\mathscr{P}_{\widetilde{\sigma}_{1}}))

(resp.
(C(\widetilde{\sigma}_{0}),C(\mathscr{P}_{\widetilde{\sigma}_{0}}))
), and the irreducible
component

Y_{\tilde{\sigma}_{1}}
(resp.
Y_{\tilde{\sigma}_{0}}
)
of the general fiber
of the corresponding mirror family is the normalization of
Y_{\sigma_{1}}
(resp.
Y_{\sigma_{0}}
). As above, a singular point of
\partial Y_{\tilde{\sigma}_{1}}
becomes smooth in
\partial Y_{\tilde{\sigma}_{0}}
. It follows that
Y_{\rho_{1}}
and
Y_{\rho_{0}}
are also non-isomorphic in this case, and this concludes the proof that the map

\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}}^{\mathrm{sec}}\to\mathcal{M}_{(Y,D,L)}
is finite.

To complete the proof of Theorem
8.11
, it remains to show that the map

\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}}\to\mathcal{M}_{(Y,D,L)}
is surjective.
The moduli stack of log Calabi–Yau surfaces deformation equivalent to
(Y,D)
is irreducible of dimension
Q-2
, where
Q
is the charge of
(Y,D)
as in
[
34
, Definition 1.1]
. Indeed, fixing a toric model
(\overline{Y},\overline{D})
, this space is covered by the torus
(\mathbb{C}^{\star})^{Q}
of positions of the points to blow-ups on
\overline{D}
, quotiented by the action of
(\mathbb{C}^{\star})^{2}
by toric automorphisms. Moreover, given such a log Calabi–Yau pair
(Y^{\prime},D^{\prime})
, the space of curves
C^{\prime}\in|L^{\prime}|
such that
(Y^{\prime},D^{\prime}+\epsilon\,C^{\prime})
is a KSBA stable pair, is open, and so dense in
|L^{\prime}|
if non-empty.
Hence, the space of
KSBA stable pairs deformation equivalent to
(Y,D,L)
is irreducible, and so the closure

\mathcal{M}_{(Y,D,L)}
of this space in the moduli space of all KSBA stable pairs is also irreducible. On the other hand, the secondary fan
\mathrm{Sec}(\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathscr{P}})
is complete, and so the toric stack
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}}
is proper. Therefore, it is enough to prove that the map
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}}\to\mathcal{M}_{(Y,D,L)}
is dominant.

Fix a large enough positive integer
k
and a refinement
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}
of the scaled polyhedral decomposition
kP
as in Lemma
8.3
. By Theorem
8.10
, the general fiber
(Y_{t},D_{t})
of
(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}})\rightarrow\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}}
is deformation equivalent to
(Y,D)
, and so admits a toric model
(Y_{t},D_{t})\leftarrow(\widetilde{Y}_{t},\widetilde{D}_{t})\rightarrow(\overline{Y},\overline{D})
, where
(\overline{Y},\overline{D})
is the toric variety with momentum polytope
\overline{P}
, and the exceptional curves
E_{ij}
of
\widetilde{Y}_{t}\rightarrow\overline{Y}
are in one-to-one correspondence with the cut triangles
\Delta_{ij}
. Moreover, it follows from the proof of Theorem
8.10
that
(\widetilde{Y}_{t},\widetilde{D}_{t})
admits an almost toric fibration over
(kP)^{gs}
, and that the classes of the exceptional curves
E_{ij}
are the classes in
\widetilde{Y}_{t}
obtained by lifting as in
[
71
, §6.2]
,
[
83
]

the tropical 1-cycles defined by the cuts
\delta_{ij}
ending at the focus-focus singularities
\widetilde{p}_{ij}
as in §
8.2
.
In particular, the period calculation proving
[
71
, Proposition 6.15]
, based on the main result of
[
83
]
, directly generalizes to our set-up and shows that the marked period of
(Y_{t},D_{t})
marked by
\mathcal{O}_{Y_{t}}(E_{ij})
, as defined in
[
42
]
, is equal to
t^{E_{ij}}
. This shows that every log Calabi–Yau surface
deformation equivalent to
(Y,D,L)
appears as a fiber
(Y_{t},D_{t},L_{t})
of
(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}})\rightarrow\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}}
. Finally, given such a fiber
(Y_{t},D_{t},L_{t})
, it follows from the torus action given by Lemma
6.27
that for every
\mathbf{a}:=(a_{p})_{p\in P_{\mathbb{Z}}}\in(\mathbb{C}^{\star})^{P_{\mathbb{Z}}}
, denoting by
C_{\mathbf{a}}
the curve in
Y_{t}
defined by
the equation
\sum_{p\in P_{\mathbb{Z}}}a_{p}\vartheta_{p}=0
, the pair
(Y_{t},D_{t}+\epsilon\,C_{\mathbf{a}})
appears as a fiber of
(\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}}+\epsilon\,\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}})\rightarrow\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}}
. Since
(\vartheta_{p})_{p\in P_{\mathbb{Z}}}
is a basis of
H^{0}(Y_{t},L_{t})
, the set of curves of the form
C_{\mathbf{a}}
is open dense in the linear system
|L_{t}|
.
Hence, we obtain that an open dense subset of
\mathcal{M}_{(Y,D,L)}
is contained in the image of
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{sec}}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{(Y,D,L)}
, and this concludes the proof of Theorem
8.11
.
∎

Corollary 8.12
.

The complete toric variety
S_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}}^{\mathrm{sec}}
with fan the secondary fan
\mathrm{Sec}(\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}/\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathscr{P}})
is projective.

Proof.

By Theorem
8.11
,
S_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{P}}}^{\mathrm{sec}}
is a finite cover of the coarse KSBA moduli space
M_{(Y,D,L)}
, which is projective by
[
36
,
69
]
.
∎
